Computation of Weighted Bergman Inner Products on Bounded Symmetric Domains and Parseval–Plancherel-Type Formulas under Subgroups
Ryosuke NAKAHAMA ab

Abstract
Let ( G , G 1 ) = ( G , ( G σ ) 0 ) (G,G_{1})=(G,(G^{\sigma})_{0}) be a symmetric pair of holomorphic type, and we consider a pair of Hermitian symmetric spaces D 1 = G 1 / K 1 ⊂ D = G / K D_{1}=G_{1}/K_{1}\subset D=G/K , realized as bounded symmetric domains in complex vector spaces 𝔭 1 + := ( 𝔭 + ) σ ⊂ 𝔭 + \mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}:=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{\sigma}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+} respectively. Then the universal covering group G ~ \widetilde{G} of G G acts unitarily on the weighted Bergman space ℋ λ ​ ( D ) ⊂ 𝒪 ⁡ ( D ) = 𝒪 λ ​ ( D ) \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)\subset\mathcal{O}(D)=\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D) on D D for sufficiently large λ \lambda . Its restriction to the subgroup G ~ 1 \widetilde{G}_{1} decomposes discretely and multiplicity-freely, and its branching law is given explicitly by Hua–Kostant–Schmid–Kobayashi’s formula in terms of the K ~ 1 \widetilde{K}_{1} -decomposition of the space 𝒫 ⁡ ( 𝔭 2 + ) \mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr) of polynomials on 𝔭 2 + := ( 𝔭 + ) − σ ⊂ 𝔭 + \mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}:=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+} . The object of this article is to understand the decomposition of the restriction ℋ λ ​ ( D ) | G ~ 1 \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}} by studying the weighted Bergman inner product on each K ~ 1 \widetilde{K}_{1} -type in 𝒫 ⁡ ( 𝔭 2 + ) ⊂ ℋ λ ​ ( D ) \mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D) . For example, by computing explicitly the norm ‖ f ‖ λ \|f\|_{\lambda} for f = f ⁡ ( x 2 ) ∈ 𝒫 ⁡ ( 𝔭 2 + ) f=f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr) , we can determine the Parseval–Plancherel-type formula for the decomposition of ℋ λ ​ ( D ) | G ~ 1 \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}} . Also, by computing the poles of ⟨ f ⁡ ( x 2 ) , e ( x | z ¯ ) 𝔭 + ⟩ λ , x \bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x} for f ⁡ ( x 2 ) ∈ 𝒫 ⁡ ( 𝔭 2 + ) f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr) , x = ( x 1 , x 2 ) x=(x_{1},x_{2}) , z ∈ 𝔭 + = 𝔭 1 + ⊕ 𝔭 2 + z\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2} , we can get some information on branching of 𝒪 λ ​ ( D ) | G ~ 1 \mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}} also for λ \lambda in non-unitary range. In this article we consider these problems for all K ~ 1 \widetilde{K}_{1} -types in 𝒫 ⁡ ( 𝔭 2 + ) \mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr) .



keywords
weighted Bergman spaces; holomorphic discrete series representations; branching laws; Parseval–Plancherel-type formulas; highest weight modules

1
Introduction

The purpose of this article is to study the restriction of holomorphic discrete series representations to some subgroups,
e.g., to determine the Parseval–Plancherel-type formulas,
by computing the weighted Bergman inner products on bounded symmetric domains.
This article is a continuation of the author’s previous articles
[
43
,
44
]
.

We consider a Hermitian symmetric space
D\simeq G/K
, realized as a bounded symmetric domain
D\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
in a complex vector space
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
centered at the origin.
We assume that
G
is connected, and
K
is the isotropy subgroup at
0\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
.
Let
\widetilde{G}
,
\widetilde{K}
denote the universal covering groups of
G
,
K
, let
(\tau,V)
be a finite-dimensional representation of
\widetilde{K}
,
and we consider the homogeneous vector bundle
\widetilde{G}\times_{\widetilde{K}}V\to\widetilde{G}/\widetilde{K}\simeq D
.
Then we can trivialize this bundle, and the space of holomorphic sections is identified with the space
\mathcal{O}(D,V)=\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)
of
V
-valued holomorphic functions on
D
,
on which
\widetilde{G}
acts by

(\hat{\tau}(g)f)(x):=\tau\bigl(\kappa\bigl(g^{-1},x\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}f\bigl(g^{-1}x\bigr),\qquad g\in\widetilde{G},\quad x\in D,\quad f\in\mathcal{O}(D,V),

by using some function
\tau\colon\widetilde{G}\times D\to\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
.
According to the choice of the representation
(\tau,V)
of
\widetilde{K}
, there may or may not exist a Hilbert subspace
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)

on which
\widetilde{G}
acts unitarily. The classification of
(\tau,V)
such that
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)
exists is given by
[
10
,
21
]
.
Especially, if the
\widetilde{G}
-invariant inner product of
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)
is given by an integral on
D
,
then this is called a
weighted Bergman inner product
and
(\hat{\tau},\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V))
is called a
holomorphic discrete series representation
.
For example, suppose
G
is simple and
(\tau,V)=\bigl(\chi^{-\lambda},\mathbb{C}\bigr)
is one-dimensional, where
\chi
is a suitably normalized character of
K
.
Then for sufficiently large
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, the weighted Bergman inner product is given by the integral of the form

\langle f,g\rangle_{\lambda}:=C_{\lambda}\int_{D}f(x)\overline{g(x)}h(x,\overline{x})^{\lambda-p}\,{\rm d}x,

where
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
,
h(x,\overline{y})
is a suitable polynomial on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\overline{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
,
and the corresponding reproducing kernel (
weighted Bergman kernel
) is given by
h(x,\overline{y})^{-\lambda}
, if we choose the normalizing constant
C_{\lambda}
suitably.
In this case we write
(\hat{\tau},\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V))=:(\tau_{\lambda},\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D))
, and call it a holomorphic discrete series representation of
scalar type
.

Let us give a concrete example. We consider

G={\rm U}(q,s):=\left\{g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in{\rm GL}(q+s,\mathbb{C})\ \middle|\ g^{*}\begin{pmatrix}I_{q}&\hphantom{-}0\\
0&-I_{s}\end{pmatrix}g=\begin{pmatrix}I_{q}&\hphantom{-}0\\
0&-I_{s}\end{pmatrix}\right\}.

Then
G
acts transitively on

D:=\{x\in{\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C})\mid I-xx^{*}\text{ is positive definite}\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}:={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C})

by the linear fractional transform

\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}.x:=(ax+b)(cx+d)^{-1}=(a^{*}+xb^{*})^{-1}(c^{*}+xd^{*}).

Next let
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
. Then the universal covering group
\widetilde{G}
acts on
\mathcal{O}(D)
by

\left(\tau_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\right)f\right)(x):=\operatorname{det}(a^{*}+xb^{*})^{-\lambda_{1}}\operatorname{det}(cx+d)^{-\lambda_{2}}f\bigl((ax+b)(cx+d)^{-1}\bigr).

We note that
\operatorname{det}(a^{*}+xb^{*})^{-\lambda_{1}}\operatorname{det}(cx+d)^{-\lambda_{2}}
is not well-defined on
G\times D
unless
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
but is well-defined on the universal covering space
\widetilde{G}\times D
.
If
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfies
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}>q+s-1
, then this preserves the weighted Bergman inner product

\langle f,g\rangle_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}:=C_{\lambda}\int_{D}f(x)\overline{g(x)}\operatorname{det}(I-xx^{*})^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}-(q+s)}\,{\rm d}x,

and the corresponding Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}(D)\subset\mathcal{O}(D)
gives a holomorphic discrete series representation of scalar type.
The restriction of
\tau_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}
to the subgroup
{\rm SU}(q,s)
depends only on the sum
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}
.

Next we consider an involution
\sigma
on
G
, and let
G_{1}:=(G^{\sigma})_{0}
be the identity component of the group of fixed points by
\sigma
.
Without loss of generality we may assume
\sigma
stabilizes
K
, and let
K_{1}:=G_{1}\cap K
.
Then
D_{1}:=G_{1}.0\simeq G_{1}/K_{1}
is either a complex submanifold or a totally real submanifold of
D\simeq G/K
.
The pair
(G,G_{1})
is called a symmetric pair of
holomorphic type
for the former case, and of
anti-holomorphic type
for the latter case (see
[
26
, Section 3.4]
).
In the following we assume
(G,G_{1})
is of holomorphic type. Then
\sigma
induces a holomorphic action on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\simeq T_{0}(G/K)
,
and let
(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{\sigma}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}
,
(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
, so that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
holds.
Now we consider the restriction of a holomorphic discrete series representation
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)
of
\widetilde{G}
to the subgroup
\widetilde{G}_{1}
.
Then the restriction
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
decomposes into a Hilbert direct sum of irreducible representations of
\widetilde{G}_{1}
,
and this decomposition is given in terms of the decomposition of
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\otimes(V|_{\widetilde{K}_{1}})
under
\widetilde{K}_{1}
,
where
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
denotes the space of polynomials on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
. That is, if
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\otimes(V|_{\widetilde{K}_{1}})
is decomposed under
\widetilde{K}_{1}
as

\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\otimes(V|_{\widetilde{K}_{1}})\simeq\bigoplus_{j}m(\tau,\rho_{j})(\rho_{j},W_{j}),\qquad m(\tau,\rho_{j})\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},

then
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
is decomposed abstractly into the Hilbert direct sum

\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{j}m(\tau,\rho_{j})\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j})

(see Kobayashi
[
26
, Lemma 8.8]
,
[
25
, Section 8]
, for earlier results, see also
[
22
,
39
]
).
We note that if the unitary subrepresentation
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)
is not holomorphic discrete,
then its
\widetilde{K}
-finite part
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)_{\widetilde{K}}
may be strictly smaller than
\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)_{\widetilde{K}}=\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\otimes V
,
and the above decomposition may not hold in general.

Let us observe this decomposition when
G
is simple and
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is a holomorphic discrete series representation of scalar type.
In general,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
has a
Jordan triple system
structure, and if
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple, then for an involution
\sigma
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,

\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}
is a direct sum of at most 2 simple Jordan triple subsystems.
Let
\chi
,
\chi_{1}
be suitable characters of
\widetilde{K}
,
\widetilde{K}_{1}
, respectively, and let
\chi|_{\widetilde{K}_{1}}=\chi_{1}^{\varepsilon_{1}}
, where
\varepsilon_{1}\in\{1,2\}
.
Then the decomposition of
\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
under
\widetilde{K}_{1}
,

\bigl(\chi^{-\lambda}|_{\widetilde{K}_{1}}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\simeq\chi^{-\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\otimes\bigoplus_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)

is given by using the parameter set

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}:=\{(k_{1},\dots,k_{r_{2}})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r_{2}}\mid k_{1}\geq\cdots\geq k_{r_{2}}\geq 0\},\qquad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\text{simple},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\text{non-simple}

for some
r_{2},r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. According to this decomposition,
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is decomposed under
\widetilde{G}_{1}
as

\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

(1.1)

(see Kobayashi
[
26
, Theorem 8.3]
), where
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)
denotes the holomorphic discrete series representation of
\widetilde{G}_{1}

with the fiber
\chi_{1}^{-\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
. Each
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)
-isotypic component in
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)

is generated by
\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
as a
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-module.
Our aim is to understand this decomposition concretely by studying the weighted Bergman inner product

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x},\qquad x=(x_{1},x_{2}),\quad z\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2},\quad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr).

(1.2)

Here the subscript
x
stands for the variable of integration.

One of main problems on the restriction of representations is to determine the
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-intertwining operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}\colon\

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j})\quad

or

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\uparrow}\colon\

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\rho}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\quad

or

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}.

\mathcal{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}
is called a
symmetry breaking operator
and
\mathcal{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\uparrow}
is called a
holographic operator
,
according to the terminology introduced in
[
32
,
33
]
and
[
34
]
respectively.
Such a problem is proposed by Kobayashi from the viewpoint of the representation theory (see
[
28
]
),
and studied from various viewpoints, e.g., by
[
6
,
7
,
8
,
19
,
23
,
27
,
29
,
31
,
32
,
33
,
34
,
35
,
36
,
42
,
43
,
44
,
49
,
50
,
51
,
53
]

for holomorphic discrete series, principal series, and complementary series representations.
Especially, it is proved by
[
27
,
32
]
that in the holomorphic setting, symmetry breaking operators
\mathcal{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j})

are always given by differential operators, and their symbols are characterized as polynomial solutions of certain systems of differential equations (F-method).
Also, by
[
43
]
when
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)
is holomorphic discrete, the differential operator

\tilde{\mathcal{F}}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\rho_{j}}(D_{1},W_{j}),\qquad f(x)=f(x_{1},x_{2})\longmapsto\tilde{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\right)f(x)\biggr|_{x_{2}=0}

defined by using the operator-valued polynomial
\tilde{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}(z)\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})\otimes\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(V,W_{j})
on the dual space
\mathfrak{p}^{-}
of
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,

\tilde{F}_{\tau\rho_{j}}^{\downarrow}(z)=\bigl\langle{\rm e}^{(x|z)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}},\mathrm{K}(x_{2})\bigr\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V),x},\qquad\mathrm{K}(x_{2})\in\bigl(\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\otimes\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}\bigl(\overline{V},\overline{W_{j}}\bigr)\bigr)^{\widetilde{K}_{1}}

becomes a symmetry breaking operator. Here
(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
is a suitable non-degenerate pairing on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-}
.
Especially when
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is of scalar type, the construction of symmetry breaking operators

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

is reduced to the computation of (
1.2
).
Since the restriction of a unitary highest weight representation of scalar type
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
decomposes multiplicity-freely by
[
26
, Theorem A]
,
symmetry breaking operators from
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
are unique up to constant multiple.
On the other hand, if
\lambda
is not in the holomorphic discrete range, we do not know a priori whether symmetry breaking operators from
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
are unique or not.
Indeed, for tensor product case, the space of symmetry breaking operators from
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\mathcal{O}_{\mu}(D)
may become greater than 1-dimensional for some
(\lambda,\mu)\in\mathbb{C}

(see
[
33
, Section 9]
, or Section
8
of this paper).

In
[
44
]
, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple and when both
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
are of tube type, we explicitly computed the inner product (
1.2
) for
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}
of the form one of

\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{k}=(k+l,k,\dots,k)
,
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{k}=(k+1,\allowbreak\dots,k+1,k,\dots,k)
or
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})=((k,\dots,k),\mathbf{l})
according to the choice of
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
,
and determined the symmetry breaking operators for these
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}
.
Especially if
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=(k,\dots,k)
or
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=((k,\dots,k),(l,\dots,l))
, then
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)
becomes of scalar type,
and the inner product (
1.2
) and the symmetry breaking operator are given by using Heckman–Opdam’s hypergeometric polynomials of type
BC
.
Also in
[
43
]
, we have constructed the holographic operators for some
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}
containing the above cases, as infinite-order differential operators.

We continue to assume
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is of scalar type. Then since the decomposition (
1.1
) is a Hilbert direct sum and is multiplicity-free,
under a suitable normalization of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
, there exist constants
C\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)>0
such that

\|f\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}C\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{2},\qquad f\in\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)

holds, where
\|\cdot\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}
is a
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-invariant norm on
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)
.
This formula is regarded as an analogue of the Parseval or the Plancherel theorems for the Fourier analysis.
Such Parseval–Plancherel-type formulas for symmetric pairs of holomorphic type are studied, e.g., by
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
16
,
17
,
34
]

under different realizations of
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
,
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)
,
and those for anti-holomorphic type cases are studied, e.g., by
[
40
,
45
,
46
,
47
,
48
,
56
,
57
,
58
,
60
,
61
,
62
,
63
]
.
In our setting, for
(G,G_{1})
of holomorphic type, each
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)
-isotypic component in
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is generated by the minimal
\widetilde{K}_{1}
-type

\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
, and we assume that
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
is normalized such that

\bigl\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\bigr\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}
is independent of the parameter
\lambda
for
f=f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
.
Under this setting we want to determine the
\lambda
-dependence of
C\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)
explicitly.
To do this, it is enough to compute
\|f\|_{\lambda}
for
f=f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
for each
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}
,
and this is deduced from the top term, i.e., the value at
z_{1}=0
, of the inner product (
1.2
) (see Proposition
2.6
).

Next we consider
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)
for
\lambda
not in the holomorphic discrete range.
Following Faraut–Korányi
[
12
,
13
]
, the weighted Bergman inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
is explicitly computed
on each
\widetilde{K}
-type in
\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
for sufficiently large
\lambda
,
and this is meromorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.
For smaller
\lambda
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
becomes reducible as a
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-module if
\lambda
is a pole of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
,
and for any
f\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, by computing the poles and their orders of the inner product
\bigl\langle f,{\rm e}^{(\cdot|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda}
with respect to
\lambda
,
we can determine which submodule
f
sits in. Similarly, for a symmetric subgroup
G_{1}\subset G
of holomorphic type, the decomposition (
1.1
) is restated as

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\bigr|_{(\mathfrak{g}_{1},\widetilde{K}_{1})}=\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\bigr|_{(\mathfrak{g}_{1},\widetilde{K}_{1})}=\bigoplus_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1}))\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)

for sufficiently large
\lambda
, where
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1})
denotes the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
, but this does not hold for smaller
\lambda
in general.
By computing the poles and their orders of the inner product (
1.2
) with respect to
\lambda
, we can determine which
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-submodule of
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}

contains the
\bigl(\mathfrak{g}_{1},\widetilde{K}_{1}\bigr)
-module
{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1}))\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
(see Proposition
2.7
).

In this article we treat irreducible symmetric pairs
(G,G_{1})=(G,(G^{\sigma})_{0})
of holomorphic type, and treat holomorphic discrete series representations
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
of scalar type.
According to the decomposition
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
of the space of polynomials on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}
,
we compute the top terms (Theorems
5.1
,
6.1
, and
8.4
)
and the poles (Theorems
5.2
,
6.2
, and
8.1
)
of the weighted Bergman inner product (
1.2
) for all
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
or
{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Also, we apply these results for the determination of Parseval–Plancherel-type formulas (Corollaries
4.2
,
5.7
,
6.7
, and
8.7
) and
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-modules (Corollaries
4.3
,
5.8
,
6.8
, and
8.8
).

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we review Jordan triple systems, Jordan algebras and holomorphic discrete series representations.
In Section
3
, we introduce some lemmas needed in later sections.
In Section
4
, we treat
(G,G_{1})
such that (
1.2
) is easily computable.
For these
(G,G_{1})
, the symmetry breaking operators are given by normal derivatives along
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
independent of the parameter
\lambda
,
and (
1.2
) is computed directly by using Faraut–Korányi’s result
[
12
,
13
]
.
In Section
5
, we treat the cases such that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is a direct sum of two simple Jordan triple systems,
and in Section
6
, we treat the cases such that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is simple.
However, for some exceptional
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
, we need some extra computation to determine the poles of (
1.2
).
In Section
7
, we compute the poles of (
1.2
) when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
are simple of rank 3. The above exceptional case is contained in these rank 3 cases.
Also, in this section we give a conjecture on the full computation of (
1.2
) for rank 3 cases.
In Section
8
, we treat the cases
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\bigr)=\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0},\Delta\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0}\bigr)\bigr)
, and the tensor product representation
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{0})\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\mathcal{H}_{\mu}(D_{0})
.
In this section we also discuss the higher-multiplicity phenomena, which is a generalization of the argument in
[
33
, Section 9]
.

2
Preliminaries

In this section we review Jordan triple systems, Jordan algebras and holomorphic discrete series representations.
We use almost the same notations as in
[
44
]
, and readers who read
[
44
]
can skip Sections
2.1
–
2.6
.
For detail see also, e.g.,
[
11
, Parts III and V]
and
[
13
,
38
,
54
]
.

2.1
Hermitian positive Jordan triple systems

We consider a Hermitian positive Jordan triple system
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{-},\{\cdot,\cdot,\cdot\},\overline{\cdot}\bigr)
,
where
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
are finite-dimensional vector spaces over
\mathbb{C}
, with a non-degenerate bilinear form
(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}\to\mathbb{C}
,

\{\cdot,\cdot,\cdot\}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\to\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-trilinear map satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{x,y,z\}=\{z,y,x\},\\
&\{u,v,\{x,y,z\}\}=\{\{u,v,x\},y,z\}-\{x,\{v,u,y\},z\}+\{x,y,\{u,v,z\}\},\\
&(\{u,v,x\}|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}=(x|\{v,u,y\})_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\end{split}

for any
u,x,z\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
,
v,y\in\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}
, and
\overline{\cdot}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\to\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-antilinear involutive isomorphism
satisfying
(x|\overline{x})_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\geq 0
for any
x\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
.
Let
D,B\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)
,
Q\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{\mp},\mathfrak{p}^{\pm})
,
Q\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{\mp},\mathfrak{p}^{\pm})
be the maps given by

\displaystyle D(x,y)z=Q(x,z)y:=\{x,y,z\},

\displaystyle Q(x):=\frac{1}{2}Q(x,x),

\displaystyle B(x,y):=I_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}-D(x,y)+Q(x)Q(y)

for
x,z\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
,
y\in\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}
, let
h=h_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}\to\mathbb{C}
be the
generic norm
, which is a polynomial on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-}

irreducible on each simple Jordan triple subsystem, and write
B(x):=B(x,\overline{x})
,
h(x):=h(x,\overline{x})
.
If
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple, then
B(x,y)
and
h(x,y)
are related as

h(x,y)^{p}=\operatorname{Det}_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}B(x,y),\qquad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+},\quad y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}

(2.1)

for some
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

If
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is a tripotent, i.e.,
\{e,\overline{e},e\}=2e
, then
D(e,\overline{e})\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
has the eigenvalues
0
,
1
,
2
. For
j=0,1,2
, let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{j}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(\overline{e})_{j}:=\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}\mid D(e,\overline{e})x=jx\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{-}(\overline{e})_{j}=\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{j}:=\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}\mid D(\overline{e},e)x=jx\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{-},

(2.2)

so that
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}(e)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}(e)_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}(e)_{0}
holds (
Peirce decomposition
).
A non-zero tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is called
primitive
if
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}=\mathbb{C}e
, and
maximal
if
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{0}=\{0\}
.
If
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}
holds for some (or equivalently any) maximal tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, then we say that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is of
tube type
.
Throughout the paper, we assume that the bilinear form
(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\times\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}\to\mathbb{C}
is normalized such that

(e|\overline{e})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=(\overline{e}|e)_{\mathfrak{p}^{-}}=1
holds for any primitive tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
.
If
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple, then under this normalization
(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
and
D(x,y)
are related as

p(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}D(x,y),\qquad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+},\quad y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}

(2.3)

with the same
p\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
as in (
2.1
).

Next we fix a tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, and consider
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
as in (
2.2
). Then

Q(\overline{e})\colon\ \mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2},\qquad Q(e)\colon\ \mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}

are mutually inverse. For
x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
let
P(x):=Q(x)Q(\overline{e})\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2})
. Then
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
becomes a Jordan algebra with the product

x\cdot y:=\frac{1}{2}\{x,\overline{e},y\},\qquad x,y\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2},

with the unit element
e
, and with the inverse

x^{\mathit{-1}}:=P(x)^{-1}x,\qquad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}.

The Euclidean real form
\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
is given by

\mathfrak{n}^{+}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\mid Q(e)\overline{x}=x\big\}.

Let
(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\times\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\to\mathbb{C}
be the symmetric bilinear form on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}=\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}
given by

(x|y)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}:=(x|Q(\overline{e})y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=(y|Q(\overline{e})x)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}},\qquad x,y\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}.

This is positive definite on
\mathfrak{n}^{+}
. Also, let
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)
be the
determinant polynomial
on
{\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\!=\!\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}}
,
and let
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{-}}(y):=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(Q(e)y)
for
y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}
.
Then the generic norm
h(x,y)
and
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)
are related as

\displaystyle t^{r}h\bigl(t^{-1}x,y\bigr)\bigr|_{t=0}=(-1)^{r}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{-}}(y),

\displaystyle h(x,\overline{e})=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(e-x),\qquad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2},\quad y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2},

where
r:=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}=\deg\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)
. In addition, let

\displaystyle\Omega:\hskip-3.0pt

\displaystyle=(\text{connected component of }\{x\in\mathfrak{n}^{+}\mid P(x)\text{ is positive definite}\}\text{ which contains }e)

\displaystyle=(\text{connected component of }\{x\in\mathfrak{n}^{+}\mid\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)>0\}\text{ which contains }e)

be the
symmetric cone
.

2.2
Structure groups and the Kantor–Koecher–Tits construction

In this subsection we consider some Lie algebras corresponding to Jordan triple systems
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
.
For
l\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, let
\overline{l},{}^{t}\hskip-1.0ptl\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})
,
l^{*}\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
be the elements given by

\overline{l}\overline{x}=\overline{lx}
,
(lx|\overline{y})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=(x|{}^{t}\hskip-1.0ptl\overline{y})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=(x|\overline{l^{*}y})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
for
x,y\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
.
Then the
Structure group

\operatorname{Str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
and the
automorphism group

\operatorname{Aut}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
are defined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{Str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}):=\big\{l\in{\rm GL}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\,\big|\,\big\{lx,{}^{t}\hskip-1.0ptl^{-1}y,lz\big\}=l\{x,y,z\},\,x,z\in\mathfrak{p}^{+},\,y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}\big\},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Aut}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}):=\big\{k\in\operatorname{Str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\,\big|\,k^{-1}=k^{*}\big\}.

Let
\mathfrak{str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathfrak{der}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathfrak{k}
denote the Lie algebras of
\operatorname{Str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
and
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
respectively.
Then
D\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{-}\bigr)=\mathfrak{str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
holds.

Next we recall the
Kantor–Koecher–Tits construction
. As vector spaces let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}:=\big\{(x,k,\overline{x})\mid x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+},\,k\in\mathfrak{k}\big\}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}},

and give the Lie algebra structure on
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
by

[(x,k,y),(z,l,w)]:=\bigl(kz-lx,[k,l]+D(x,w)-D(z,y),-{}^{t}\hskip-1.0ptkw+{}^{t}\hskip-1.0ptly\bigr).

Then
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
becomes a Lie algebra and
\mathfrak{g}
becomes a real form of
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
{(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}}\colon\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}\times\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{C}}
be the
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
-invariant bilinear form normalized such that
(x|y)_{\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}}=(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
holds for any
{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
,
{y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}}
.
We fix a connected complex Lie group
G^{\mathbb{C}}
with the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
and let
{G,K^{\mathbb{C}},K,P^{+},P^{-}\!\subset\!G^{\mathbb{C}}}
be the connected closed subgroups corresponding to the Lie algebras
{\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}},\mathfrak{k},\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{-}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}}
, respectively.
Then
\operatorname{Ad}|_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\colon K^{\mathbb{C}}\to\operatorname{Str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})_{0}
gives a covering map, and for
l\in K^{\mathbb{C}}
,
x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, we abbreviate
\operatorname{Ad}(l)x=:lx
.

Next we fix a tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, regard
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
as a Jordan algebra, and let
\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}(e)
be the Euclidean real form. Also, let

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathfrak{n}^{-}:=\overline{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}=Q(\overline{e})\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2},\\
&\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}:=D\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2},\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}\bigr)=\big[\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2},\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}\big]\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}},\\
&\mathfrak{k}(e)_{2}:=\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}\cap\mathfrak{k}\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2},\\
&\mathfrak{l}:=D\bigl(\mathfrak{n}^{+},\mathfrak{n}^{-}\bigr)=\big[\mathfrak{n}^{+},\mathfrak{n}^{-}\big]\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2},\\
&\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}},\\
&\mathfrak{g}(e)_{2}:=\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}\cap\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2},\\
&{}^{c}\hskip-1.0pt\mathfrak{g}:=\mathfrak{n}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{l}\oplus\mathfrak{n}^{-}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}.\end{split}

These become Lie subalgebras of the right-hand sides. Let
K^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2},K(e)_{2},G(e)_{2},{}^{c}\hskip-1.0ptG\subset G^{\mathbb{C}}
be the connected closed subgroups corresponding to the Lie algebras

\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2},\mathfrak{k}(e)_{2},\mathfrak{g}(e)_{2},{}^{c}\hskip-1.0pt\mathfrak{g}\subset\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}
respectively,
and let
L:=K^{\mathbb{C}}\cap{}^{c}\hskip-1.0ptG
,
K_{L}:=L\cap K
,
\mathfrak{k}_{\mathfrak{l}}:=\mathfrak{l}\cap\mathfrak{k}
.
Then
G(e)_{2}
and
{}^{c}\hskip-1.0ptG
are isomorphic via the Cayley transform in
G^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}
,
both
K(e)_{2}
and
L
are real forms of
K^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}
,

L
acts transitively on the symmetric cone
\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}
, and
K_{L}
acts on
\mathfrak{n}^{+}
as Jordan algebra automorphisms.
Also, for
l\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}=\mathfrak{l}^{\mathbb{C}}\subset\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2})=\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}\bigl(\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}\bigr)
,
let
l^{\top}=Q(e){}^{t}\hskip-1.0ptlQ(\overline{e})\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}=\mathfrak{l}^{\mathbb{C}}
, and extend to the anti-automorphism on
K^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}=L^{\mathbb{C}}
, so that
(lx|y)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}=\bigl(x|l^{\top}y\bigr)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}
holds.

2.3
Simultaneous Peirce decomposition

In this subsection we assume
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple, or equivalently, the corresponding Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
is simple.
We fix a Jordan frame
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{r}\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, i.e., a maximal set of primitive tripotents in
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
satisfying
D(e_{i},\overline{e_{j}})=0
for
i\neq j
,
where
r=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{R}}\mathfrak{g}
. Then
e:=e_{1}+\cdots+e_{r}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
becomes a maximal tripotent,
and we take
\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{l}\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}
as in the previous subsection.
Next let
h_{j}:=D(e_{j},\overline{e_{j}})=[e_{j},\overline{e_{j}}]\in\mathfrak{l}\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}
, and let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{l}}:=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{R}h_{j},\qquad\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mathbb{C}}:=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}h_{j},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}_{ij}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\mid[h_{l},x]=\pm(\delta_{il}+\delta_{jl})x,\,l=1,\dots,r\big\},\qquad 1\leq i\leq j\leq r,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}_{0j}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\mid[h_{l},x]=\pm\delta_{jl}x,\,l=1,\dots,r\big\},\qquad 1\leq j\leq r,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{ij}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\mid[h_{l},x]=(\delta_{il}-\delta_{jl})x,\,l=1,\dots,r\big\},\qquad 1\leq i,j\leq r,\quad i\neq j,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{i0}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\mid[h_{l},x]=\delta_{il}x,\,l=1,\dots,r\big\},\qquad 1\leq i\leq r,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{0j}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\mid[h_{l},x]=-\delta_{jl}x,\,l=1,\dots,r\big\},\qquad 1\leq j\leq r,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}:=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\mid[h_{l},x]=0,\,(h_{l}|x)_{\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}}=0,\,l=1,\dots,r\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{l}_{ij}:=\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{ij}\cap\mathfrak{l},\qquad 1\leq i,j\leq r,\quad i\neq j,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{l}}:=\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}\cap\mathfrak{l},

so that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq j\leq r\\
(i,j)\neq(0,0)\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}_{ij},\qquad\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{l}}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{m}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i,j\leq r\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{ij},\qquad\mathfrak{l}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\mathfrak{m}_{\mathfrak{l}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i,j\leq r\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{l}_{ij}

hold. The decomposition of
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
is called the
simultaneous Peirce decomposition
. Using this decomposition, we define integers
(d,b,p,n)
by

\displaystyle d:=\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{ij},\quad 1\leq i<j\leq r,\qquad

\displaystyle b:=\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0j},\quad 1\leq j\leq r,

\displaystyle p:=2+d(r-1)+b,\qquad

\displaystyle n:=\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}=r+\frac{d}{2}r(r-1)+br.

(2.4)

We note that if
r=1
, then
d
is not determined uniquely, and any number is allowed.
Then
p
coincides with the one in (
2.1
) and (
2.3
).
Also we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{l}}:=\bigoplus_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\mathfrak{l}_{ij},\qquad\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{l}}^{\top}:=\bigoplus_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\mathfrak{l}_{ji},\qquad M_{L}:=\{k\in K_{L}\mid\operatorname{Ad}(k)h_{l}=0,\,l=1,\dots,r\},

and let
A_{L},N_{L},N_{L}^{\top}\subset L
be the connected closed subgroups corresponding to the Lie subalgebras
\mathfrak{a}_{\mathfrak{l}}
,
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{l}}
,
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{l}}^{\top}
respectively,
so that
M_{L}A_{L}N_{L},M_{L}A_{L}N_{L}^{\top}\subset L
are minimal parabolic subgroups.

2.4
Space of polynomials on Jordan triple systems

In this subsection we consider the space
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)
of polynomials on the Jordan triple system
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
, on which
K^{\mathbb{C}}
acts by

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Ad}|_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}(l))^{\vee}f(x)=f\bigl(l^{-1}x\bigr),\qquad

\displaystyle l\in K^{\mathbb{C}},\quad f\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}),\quad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+},

\displaystyle(\operatorname{Ad}|_{\mathfrak{p}^{-}}(l))^{\vee}f(y)=f\bigl({}^{t}ly\bigr),\qquad

\displaystyle l\in K^{\mathbb{C}},\quad f\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-}),\quad y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}.

We assume
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple, fix a Jordan frame
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{r}\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, and consider the tripotents

e^{k}:=\sum_{j=1}^{k}e_{j}
,
\check{e}^{k}:=\sum_{j=r-k+1}^{r}e_{j}
for
k=1,2,\dots,r
. We also write
e^{r}=\check{e}^{r}=:e
. Then the subalgebras

\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigl(e^{k}\bigr)_{2}=\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq j\leq k}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{ij},\qquad\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigl(\check{e}^{k}\bigr)_{2}=\bigoplus_{r-k+1\leq i\leq j\leq r}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{ij}

have Jordan algebra structures. Let
\mathfrak{n}^{+}\bigl(e^{k}\bigr)\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigl(e^{k}\bigr)_{2}
,
\mathfrak{n}^{+}\bigl(\check{e}^{k}\bigr)\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigl(\check{e}^{k}\bigr)_{2}
be the Euclidean real forms,
and we extend the determinant polynomials
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}(e^{k})}
,
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}(\check{e}^{k})}
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigl(e^{k}\bigr)_{2}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigl(\check{e}^{k}\bigr)_{2}
to polynomials on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
.
Using these, for
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
we define the functions
\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)
,
\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)
on the symmetric cone

\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}:=\mathfrak{n}^{+}(e^{r})=\mathfrak{n}^{+}(\check{e}^{r})
by

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x):=\prod_{k=1}^{r}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}(e^{k})}(x)^{m_{k}-m_{k+1}},\!\qquad\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x):=\prod_{k=1}^{r}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}(\check{e}^{k})}(x)^{m_{k}-m_{k+1}},\!\quad x\in\Omega,\!\!

(2.5)

where we set
m_{r+1}:=0
. Then these satisfy

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(man.x)={}

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(man.e)\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(a.e)\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)={\rm e}^{2t_{1}m_{1}+2t_{2}m_{2}+\cdots+2t_{r}m_{r}}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x),

\displaystyle m\in M_{L},\quad a={\rm e}^{t_{1}h_{1}+\cdots+t_{r}h_{r}}\in A_{L},\quad n\in N^{\top}_{L},

\displaystyle\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(man.x)={}

\displaystyle\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(man.e)\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)=\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(a.e)\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)={\rm e}^{2t_{1}m_{r}+t_{2}m_{r-1}+\cdots+2t_{r}m_{1}}\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x),

\displaystyle m\in M_{L},\quad a={\rm e}^{t_{1}h_{1}+\cdots+t_{r}h_{r}}\in A_{L},\quad n\in N_{L}.

Especially if

\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}:=\{\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{r})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\mid m_{1}\geq m_{2}\geq\cdots\geq m_{r}\geq 0\},

then
\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)
,
\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)
are extended to polynomials on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
.
For
\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{r})\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
, we write
\mathbf{m}^{\vee}:=(m_{r},\dots,m_{1})\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
. Then the following holds.

Lemma 2.1

(
[
13
, Proposition VII.1.6]
)
.

For
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
,
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
,
x\in\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle\check{\Delta}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\right)\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}(x)=\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(m_{j}-k_{r-j+1}+\frac{d}{2}(r-j)+1\right)_{k_{r-j+1}}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}-\mathbf{k}^{\vee}}(x).

Here we normalize the differential operator
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
on
\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
with respect to the bilinear form
(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}=(\cdot|Q(\overline{e})\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
.
Especially, if
k_{1}=\cdots=k_{r}=k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\mathbf{m}
is of the form
\mathbf{m}=(\mu+l_{1},\mu+l_{2},\dots,\mu+l_{r})
with
\mu,l_{j}\in\mathbb{C}
, then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\bigg)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{\mu}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x)=\prod_{j=1}^{r}\bigg(\mu+l_{j}-k+\frac{d}{2}(r-j)+1\bigg)_{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{\mu-k}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(-1)^{kr}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\biggl(-\mu-l_{r-j+1}-\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\biggr)_{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{\mu-k}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x).

(2.6)

Next, for
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
, let

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}):=\operatorname{span}_{\mathbb{C}}\big\{\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{m}}\bigl(l^{-1}x\bigr)\,\big|\,l\in K^{\mathbb{C}}\big\}\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{-}):=\big\{\overline{f(\overline{y})}\,\big|\,f(x)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\big\}\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-}).

Then the following holds.

Theorem 2.2

(Hua–Kostant–Schmid,
[
11
, Part III, Theorem V.2.1]
)
.

Under the
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-action,
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)
is decomposed into the sum of irreducible submodules as

\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr).

In addition, if
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is of tube type, then for

\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{r}:=\{\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\dots,m_{r})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\mid m_{1}\geq m_{2}\geq\cdots\geq m_{r}\},

let

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr):=\mathcal{P}_{(m_{1}-m_{r},m_{2}-m_{r},\dots,m_{r-1}-m_{r},0)}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\pm}}(x)^{m_{r}}\subset\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\big[\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\pm}}(x)^{-1}\big],

so that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{P}_{-\mathbf{m}^{\vee}}(\mathfrak{p}^{\mp})
holds as a
K^{\mathbb{C}}
-module.

2.5
Holomorphic discrete series representations

In this subsection we review holomorphic discrete series representations.
First we recall the bounded symmetric domain realization (Harish-Chandra realization) of the Hermitian symmetric space
G/K
via the Borel embedding,

\textstyle{G/K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{G^{\mathbb{C}}/K^{\mathbb{C}}P^{-}}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{p}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\exp}

where

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle=(\text{connected component of }\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}\mid B(x)\text{ is positive definite}\big\}\text{ which contains }0)

\displaystyle=(\text{connected component of }\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}\mid h(x)>0\big\}\text{ which contains }0).

Here we write
B(x):=B(x,\overline{x})
,
h(x):=h(x,\overline{x})
.
For
g\in G^{\mathbb{C}}
,
x\in D
, if
g\exp(x)\in P^{+}K^{\mathbb{C}}P^{-}
holds, then we write

g\exp(x)=\exp\bigl(\pi^{+}(g,x)\bigr)\kappa(g,x)\exp(\pi^{-}(g,x)),

where
\pi^{\pm}(g,x)\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
and
\kappa(g,x)\in K^{\mathbb{C}}
. Then the map
\pi^{+}\colon G\times D\to D
gives an action of
G
on
D
, and we abbreviate
\pi^{+}(g,x)=:gx
.

Next let
(\tau,V)
be an irreducible holomorphic representation of the universal covering group
\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
of
K^{\mathbb{C}}
,
with the
\widetilde{K}
-invariant inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\tau}
.
Then the universal covering group
\widetilde{G}
of
G
acts on the space
\mathcal{O}(D,V)=\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)
of
V
-valued holomorphic functions on
D
by

(\hat{\tau}(g)f)(x):=\tau\bigl(\kappa\bigl(g^{-1},x\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}f\bigl(g^{-1}\bigr),\qquad g\in\widetilde{G},\quad x\in D,\quad f\in\mathcal{O}(D,V),

where we lift the map
\kappa\colon G\times D\to K^{\mathbb{C}}
to the universal covering spaces, and represent by the same symbol
\kappa\colon\widetilde{G}\times D\to\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
.
Its differential action is given by

({\rm d}\hat{\tau}(z,k,w)f)(x)={\rm d}\tau(k-D(x,w))f(x)+\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\biggr|_{t=0}f(x-t(z+kx-Q(x)w))

(2.7)

for
z\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
k\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
w\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}
. This becomes a highest weight representation with the minimal
\widetilde{K}
-type
(\tau,V)
.
If this contains a unitary subrepresentation
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)
,
then its reproducing kernel is proportional to
\tau(B(x,\overline{y}))
, and such unitary subrepresentation is unique.
Here we lift the map
B\colon D\times\overline{D}\to\operatorname{Str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})_{0}\subset\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
to the universal covering space,
and represent by the same symbol
B\colon D\times\overline{D}\to\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
. Especially, if the
\widetilde{G}
-invariant inner product is given by the converging integral

\langle f,g\rangle_{\hat{\tau}}:=C_{\tau}\int_{D}\bigl(\tau\bigl(B(x)^{-1}\bigr)f(x),g(x)\bigr)_{\tau}\operatorname{Det}_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}(B(x))^{-1}\,{\rm d}x

(a
weighted Bergman inner product
), then
(\hat{\tau},\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V))
is called a
holomorphic discrete series representation
.
Here we normalize the Lebesgue measure
{\rm d}x
on
D\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
with respect to the inner product
(\cdot|\overline{\cdot})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
,
and determine the constant
C_{\tau}
such that
\|v\|_{\hat{\tau}}=|v|_{\tau}
holds for all constant functions
v\in V
.

Next, let
\chi\colon\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be the character of
\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
normalized such that

{\rm d}\chi([x,y])=(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}},\qquad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+},\quad y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-},

(2.8)

so that
h(x,y)=\chi(B(x,y))
holds, and we fix a representation
(\tau_{0},V)
of
K^{\mathbb{C}}
.
When
(\tau,V)
is of the form
(\tau,V)=\bigl(\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\tau_{0},V\bigr)
, we write
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D,V)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D,V)
.
In addition, if
(\tau,V)=\bigl(\chi^{-\lambda},\mathbb{C}\bigr)
, then we write
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\tau}(D,V)=\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)
and
\hat{\tau}=\tau_{\lambda}
.

In the rest of this subsection we assume
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple and
(\tau,V)=\bigl(\chi^{-\lambda},\mathbb{C}\bigr)
.
Then
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is holomorphic discrete if
\lambda>p-1
, and then the inner product is given by

\displaystyle\langle f,g\rangle_{\lambda}=\langle f,g\rangle_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}:=C_{\lambda}\int_{D}f(x)\overline{g(x)}h(x)^{\lambda-p}\,{\rm d}x,

\displaystyle C_{\lambda}:=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{r}\Gamma\bigl(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\bigr)}{\pi^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\Gamma\bigl(\lambda-\frac{n}{r}-\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\bigr)}.

(2.9)

We consider another inner product on
\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, called the
Fischer inner product
(see, e.g.,
[
13
, Section XI.1]
), defined by

\langle f,g\rangle_{F}=\langle f,g\rangle_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}:=\frac{1}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}f(x)\overline{g(x)}{\rm e}^{-(x|\overline{x})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\,{\rm d}x=\overline{g\left(\overline{\frac{\partial}{\partial x}}\right)}f(x)\biggr|_{x=0}.

(2.10)

Here
\overline{g(\overline{\cdot})}
is a holomorphic polynomial on
\mathfrak{p}^{-}
, and we normalize
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
with respect to the bilinear form

(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{+}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-}\to\mathbb{C}
. Then the following holds.

Theorem 2.3

(Faraut–Korányi,
[
12
]
and
[
11
, Part III, Corollary V.3.9]
)
.

For
\lambda>p-1
,
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
,
f\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
,
g\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, we have

\langle f,g\rangle_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\mathbf{m},d}}\langle f,g\rangle_{F},

where

(\lambda)_{\mathbf{m},d}:=\prod_{j=1}^{r}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\right)_{m_{j}},

(2.11)

and
(\lambda)_{m}:=\lambda(\lambda+1)(\lambda+2)\cdots(\lambda+m-1)
.

Since the reproducing kernel on
\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{F}
is given by
{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{y})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}
, the following holds.

Corollary 2.4
.

For
\lambda>p-1
,
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
,
f(x)\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, we have

\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{y})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\mathbf{m},d}}f(y).

Here the subscript
x
stands for the variable of integration.
By this theorem,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
is meromorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
,
and the
\widetilde{K}
-finite part
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}=\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
is reducible as a
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-module
if and only if
\lambda
is a pole of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
.
Especially, for
j=1,2,\dots,r
,
\lambda\in\frac{d}{2}(j-1)-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

M_{j}(\lambda)=M_{j}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda):=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}\\
m_{j}\leq\frac{d}{2}(j-1)-\lambda\end{subarray}}\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\subset\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}

(2.12)

gives a
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-submodule. Moreover,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
is positive definite on
\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
for
\lambda>\frac{d}{2}(r-1)
,
and on
M_{j}^{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\bigr)
for
\lambda=\frac{d}{2}(j-1)
,
j=1,\dots,r
, that is,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
contains a unitary submodule
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)

if
\lambda
sits in the
Wallach set

\lambda\in\left\{0,\frac{d}{2},d,\dots,\frac{d}{2}(r-1)\right\}\cup\left(\frac{d}{2}(r-1),\infty\right),

(2.13)

and its
\widetilde{K}
-finite part is given by

\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}=\begin{cases}\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}),&\lambda>\frac{d}{2}(r-1),\\
M_{j}^{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\bigr),&\lambda=\frac{d}{2}(j-1),\ j=1,2,\dots,r.\end{cases}

In addition, the quotient module
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}/M_{r}(\lambda)
,
\lambda\in\frac{d}{2}(r-1)-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, also gives an infinitesimally unitary module (see
[
12
]
).
Also by the corollary, for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
,
f(x)\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
,
(\lambda)_{\mathbf{k},d}\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{y})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}
is
holomorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
if and only if

f(x)\in\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}\\
m_{j}\leq k_{j},\,j=1,\dots,r\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})

holds, and if this is satisfied, then for
j=1,\dots,r
,

f(x)\in M_{j}(\lambda)\qquad\text{holds if}\quad\lambda\in\frac{d}{2}(j-1)-k_{j}-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

2.6
Classification

A simple Hermitian positive Jordan triple system
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
is isomorphic to one of the following:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\mathbb{C}^{n},\quad n\neq 2,\qquad

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\qquad

\displaystyle{\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\qquad

\displaystyle\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\qquad

\displaystyle{\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}.

Here
\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C})
and
\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C})
denote the spaces of symmetric and alternating matrices over
\mathbb{C}
, respectively,
and
\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}
denotes the complexification of the space of
3\times 3
Hermitian matrices over the octonions
\mathbb{O}
.
Then the corresponding Lie groups
G
and their maximal compact subgroups
K
are given by

(G,K)=\begin{cases}({\rm SO}_{0}(2,n),{\rm SO}(2)\times{\rm SO}(n)),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\mathbb{C}^{n},\\
({\rm Sp}(r,\mathbb{R}),{\rm U}(r)),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\\
({\rm SU}(q,s),{\rm S}({\rm U}(q)\times{\rm U}(s))),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),\\
({\rm SO}^{*}(2s),{\rm U}(s)),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\\
(E_{7(-25)},{\rm U}(1)\times E_{6}),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\\
(E_{6(-14)},{\rm U}(1)\times{\rm Spin}(10)),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}={\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}\end{cases}

(up to covering), and the numbers
(n,r,d,b,p)
(see (
2.4
)) are given by

(n,r,d,b,p)=\begin{cases}(n,2,n-2,0,n),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\mathbb{C}^{n},\ n\geq 3,\\
(1,1,-,0,2),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\mathbb{C},\\
\left(\frac{1}{2}r(r+1),r,1,0,r+1\right),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\\
(qs,\min\{q,s\},2,|q-s|,q+s),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),\\
\left(\frac{1}{2}s(s-1),\frac{s}{2},4,0,2(s-1)\right),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\ s\colon\text{even},\\
\left(\frac{1}{2}s(s-1),\left\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\right\rfloor,4,2,2(s-1)\right),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\ s\colon\text{odd},\\
(27,3,8,0,18),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\\
(16,2,6,4,12),&\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}={\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}.\end{cases}

Here, if
r=1
, then
d
is not determined uniquely, and any number is allowed.
When
b=0
,
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
is of tube type, and has a Jordan algebra structure. That is,
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
{\rm M}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
\operatorname{Alt}(2r,\mathbb{C})
and
\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}
are of tube type.
For these cases
p=\frac{2n}{r}=d(r-1)+2
holds. When
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C})
we also consider
(G,K)=({\rm U}(q,s),{\rm U}(q)\times{\rm U}(s))
instead of
({\rm SU}(q,s),{\rm S}({\rm U}(q)\times{\rm U}(s)))
.

Next we fix the parametrization of finite-dimensional irreducible representations of
\widetilde{{\rm GL}}(s,\mathbb{C})
and
{\rm Spin}(n,\mathbb{C})
,
n\geq 3
.
We take a basis
\{\epsilon_{j}\}_{j=1}^{s}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{\mathbb{C}\vee}
of the dual space of a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}^{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathfrak{gl}(s,\mathbb{C})

such that the positive root system is given by
\{\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{j}\mid 1\leq i<j\leq s\}
.
For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{C}^{s}
with
m_{j}-m_{j+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)}
be the irreducible representation of
\widetilde{{\rm GL}}(s,\mathbb{C})
with the highest weight
\sum_{j}m_{j}\epsilon_{j}
,
and let
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)\vee}
be that with the lowest weight
-\sum_{j}m_{j}\epsilon_{j}
.
If
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{r}
, then
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)}
,
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)\vee}
are reduced to the representations of
{\rm GL}(s,\mathbb{C})
.
Similarly, we take a basis
\{\epsilon_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\subset\mathfrak{h}^{\mathbb{C}\vee}
of the dual space of a Cartan subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}^{\mathbb{C}}\subset\mathfrak{so}(n,\mathbb{C})

such that the positive root system is given by

\displaystyle\{\epsilon_{i}\pm\epsilon_{j}\mid 1\leq i<j\leq n/2\},

\displaystyle n\colon\text{even},

\displaystyle\{\epsilon_{i}\pm\epsilon_{j}\mid 1\leq i<j\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor\}\cup\{\epsilon_{j}\mid 1\leq j\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor\},\qquad

\displaystyle n\colon\text{odd}.

For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\cup\left(\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
with
m_{1}\geq\cdots\geq m_{n/2-1}\geq|m_{n/2}|
for even
n
,

m_{1}\geq\cdots\geq m_{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}\geq 0
for odd
n
, let
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{[n]}
be the irreducible representation of
{\rm Spin}(n,\mathbb{C})
with the highest weight
\sum_{j}m_{j}\epsilon_{j}
,
and let
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{[n]\vee}
be that with the lowest weight
-\sum_{j}m_{j}\epsilon_{j}
.
If
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\lfloor n/2\rfloor}
, then
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{[n]}
,
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{[n]\vee}
are reduced to the representations of
{\rm SO}(n,\mathbb{C})
.
Under this notation, the
\widetilde{K}
-type decompositions of the holomorphic discrete series representations of scalar type

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}=\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\bigoplus_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})

are given as

\displaystyle\chi^{-\lambda}\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}\boxtimes V_{(0,\dots,0)}^{[n]\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathbb{C}^{n},\ n\geq 3,\\
V_{(\lambda,\dots,\lambda)}^{(r)\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\\
V_{\left(\frac{s\lambda}{q+s},\dots,\frac{s\lambda}{q+s}\right)}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes V_{\left(\frac{q\lambda}{q+s},\dots,\frac{q\lambda}{q+s}\right)}^{(s)},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),\\
V_{\left(\frac{\lambda}{2},\dots,\frac{\lambda}{2}\right)}^{(s)\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\\
\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}\boxtimes V_{(0,\dots,0)}^{[10]\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{O}},\end{cases}

(2.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}_{-m_{1}-m_{2}}\boxtimes V_{(m_{1}-m_{2},0,\dots,0)}^{[n]\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathbb{C}^{n},\ n\geq 3,\\
V_{(2m_{1},\dots,2m_{r})}^{(r)\vee}=:V_{2\mathbf{m}}^{(r)\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\\
V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes V_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{q},0,\dots,0)}^{(s)}=:V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),\ q\leq s,\\
V_{(m_{1},\dots,m_{s},0,\dots,0)}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)}=:V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),\ q\geq s,\\
V_{(m_{1},m_{1},m_{2},m_{2},\dots,m_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor},m_{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}(,0))}^{(s)\vee}=:V_{\mathbf{m}^{2}}^{(s)\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\\
\mathbb{C}_{-\frac{3}{4}|\mathbf{m}|}\boxtimes V_{\left(\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}}{2},\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{2},\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{2},\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{2},\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{2}\right)}^{[10]\vee},&\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\end{cases}

when
K^{\mathbb{C}}
is classical, if we normalize the representations
\mathbb{C}_{-\lambda}
of
\widetilde{{\rm SO}}(2)\simeq\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)
for the first and the last cases suitably.
When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
with
n=1,2
, we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\rm SO}}_{0}(2,1)\simeq\widetilde{{\rm SL}}(2,\mathbb{R})=\widetilde{{\rm Sp}}(1,\mathbb{R}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\rm SO}}_{0}(2,2)\simeq\widetilde{{\rm SL}}(2,\mathbb{R})\times\widetilde{{\rm SL}}(2,\mathbb{R})=\widetilde{{\rm Sp}}(1,\mathbb{R})\times\widetilde{{\rm Sp}}(1,\mathbb{R}),

and we write

\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{{\rm SO}_{0}(2,1)}):=\mathcal{H}_{2\lambda}(D_{{\rm SL}(2,\mathbb{R})}),\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{{\rm SO}_{0}(2,2)}):=\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{{\rm SL}(2,\mathbb{R})})\mathbin{\hat{\boxtimes}}\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{{\rm SL}(2,\mathbb{R})}),

and similar for
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)
. When we consider
\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C})
,
G={\rm U}(q,s)
, for
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
,
let
\chi^{-\lambda_{1},-\lambda_{2}}
be the character of
\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}=\widetilde{{\rm GL}}(q,\mathbb{C})\times\widetilde{{\rm GL}}(s,\mathbb{C})
given by

\chi^{-\lambda_{1},-\lambda_{2}}\simeq V_{(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{1})}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes V_{(\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{2})}^{(s)},

and for a fixed representation
V^{\prime}\boxtimes V^{\prime\prime}
of
K^{\mathbb{C}}
, let
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}(D,V^{\prime}\boxtimes V^{\prime\prime})\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}(D,V^{\prime}\boxtimes V^{\prime\prime})
be the
representations of
\widetilde{G}
with the minimal
\widetilde{K}
-type
\chi^{-\lambda_{1},-\lambda_{2}}\otimes(V^{\prime}\boxtimes V^{\prime\prime})
.

The inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
of the holomorphic discrete series representation
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
of scalar type originally converges for
\lambda>p-1
,
and by Theorem
2.3
, this is meromorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
. This has poles at
\lambda\in\frac{d}{2}(j-1)-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
for
j=1,2,\dots,r
,
and then
M_{j}(\lambda)=M_{j}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
defined in (
2.12
) becomes a
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-submodule.
Especially, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathbb{C}^{n}
with
n\geq 3
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is reducible if and only if
\lambda\in-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\cap\left(\frac{n-2}{2}-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\right)
,
and then we have the
\bigl(\mathfrak{so}(2,n),\widetilde{{\rm SO}}(2)\times{\rm SO}(n)\bigr)
-submodules

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset M_{1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},\qquad

\displaystyle n\colon\text{even},\ \lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ \lambda\leq 0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},

\displaystyle\textstyle n\colon\text{even},\ \lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ 1\leq\lambda\leq\frac{n-2}{2},

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},

\displaystyle n\colon\text{odd},\ \lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ \lambda\leq 0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},

\displaystyle\textstyle n\colon\text{odd},\ \lambda\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2},\ \lambda\leq\frac{n-2}{2}.

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C})
with
r\geq 2
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is reducible if and only if
\lambda\in\frac{1}{2}(r-1)-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
and then we have the
(\mathfrak{sp}(r,\mathbb{R}),\widetilde{{\rm U}}(r))
-submodules

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2\left\lceil\frac{r}{2}\right\rceil-1}(\lambda)\supset M_{2\left\lceil\frac{r}{2}\right\rceil-3}(\lambda)\supset\cdots\supset M_{\max\{2\lambda,0\}+1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},\qquad

\displaystyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z},

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2\left\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\right\rfloor}(\lambda)\supset M_{2\left\lfloor\frac{r}{2}\right\rfloor-2}(\lambda)\supset\cdots\supset M_{\max\{2\lambda,1\}+1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},\qquad

\displaystyle\textstyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}.

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C})
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is reducible if and only if
\lambda\in\min\{q,s\}-1-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
and then we have the
(\mathfrak{su}(q,s),{\rm S}({\rm U}(q)\times{\rm U}(s))^{\sim})
-submodules

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{\min\{q,s\}}(\lambda)\supset M_{\min\{q,s\}-1}(\lambda)\supset\cdots\supset M_{\max\{\lambda,0\}+1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\}.

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is reducible if and only if
\lambda\in 2\left(\left\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\right\rfloor-1\right)-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
and then we have the
\bigl(\mathfrak{so}^{*}(2s),\widetilde{{\rm U}}(s)\bigr)
-submodules

\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{\left\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\right\rfloor}(\lambda)\supset M_{\left\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\right\rfloor-1}(\lambda)\supset\cdots\supset M_{\max\left\{\left\lceil\frac{\lambda}{2}\right\rceil,0\right\}+1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\}.

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is reducible if and only if
\lambda\in 8-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and then we have the

\bigl(\mathfrak{e}_{7(-25)},\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)\times E_{6}\bigr)
-submodules

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{3}(\lambda)\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset M_{1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},\qquad

\displaystyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ \lambda\leq 0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{3}(\lambda)\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},

\displaystyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ 1\leq\lambda\leq 4,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{3}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},

\displaystyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ 5\leq\lambda\leq 8.

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}={\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is reducible if and only if
\lambda\in 3-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and then we have the

\bigl(\mathfrak{e}_{6(-14)},\widetilde{{\rm U}}(1)\times{\rm Spin}(10)\bigr)
-submodules

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset M_{1}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},\qquad

\displaystyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ \lambda\leq 0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\supset M_{2}(\lambda)\supset\{0\},

\displaystyle\lambda\in\mathbb{Z},\ 1\leq\lambda\leq 3.

2.7
Restriction to symmetric subgroups

In this subsection we consider a
\mathbb{C}
-linear involution
\sigma
on a Hermitian positive Jordan triple system
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
, i.e.,
a Jordan triple system automorphism
\sigma\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\to\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
of order 2 which commutes with the
\mathbb{C}
-antilinear map
\overline{\cdot}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\to\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}
,
and extend to the involution of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-}
by letting
\sigma
act on
\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}=\mathfrak{str}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\subset\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
by

\sigma(l):=\sigma l\sigma
. Also let
{\vartheta:=-I_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}+I_{\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}}-I_{\mathfrak{p}^{-}}}
. Using these, we set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}_{1}:=\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)^{\sigma}=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\mid\sigma(x)=x\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}_{2}:=\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)^{-\sigma}=\big\{x\in\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\mid\sigma(x)=-x\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}:=\bigl(\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\sigma}=\big\{l\in\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\mid\sigma(l)=l\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{k}_{1}:=\mathfrak{k}^{\sigma}=\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}\cap\mathfrak{k},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}:=\bigl(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\sigma}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{1},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}:=\bigl(\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)^{\sigma\vartheta}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{2},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{1}:=\mathfrak{g}^{\sigma}=\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}\cap\mathfrak{g},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{2}:=\mathfrak{g}^{\sigma\vartheta}=\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}_{2}\cap\mathfrak{g},

and let
G_{1},G_{1}^{\mathbb{C}},G_{2},G_{2}^{\mathbb{C}},K_{1},K_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\subset G^{\mathbb{C}}
be the connected closed subgroups corresponding to
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{2}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathfrak{k}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{k}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
, respectively.
Such
(G,G_{1})
is called a symmetric pair of holomorphic type (see
[
26
, Section 3.4]
).
Also let
\widetilde{G}_{1}\subset\widetilde{G}
and
\widetilde{K}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\subset\widetilde{K}^{\mathbb{C}}
be the connected closed subgroups of the universal covering groups of

G
and
K^{\mathbb{C}}
corresponding to
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{k}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
respectively, and let
D\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
D_{1}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}
be the corresponding bounded symmetric domains,
so that
D\simeq G/K
,
D_{1}\simeq G_{1}/K_{1}
hold.
Let
\chi
be the character of
K^{\mathbb{C}}
given in (
2.8
). Similarly, we normalize the bilinear form

(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j}}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j}\times\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{j}\to\mathbb{C}

(j=1,2)
such that
(e|\overline{e})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j}}=1
holds for any primitive tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j}
,
and define the characters
\chi_{j}

(j=1,2)
of
K_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
by

\displaystyle{\rm d}\chi_{j}([x,y])=(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j}},\qquad

\displaystyle x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j},\ y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{j},

\displaystyle{\rm d}\chi_{j}(l)=0,

\displaystyle l\in\big[\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j},\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{j}\big]^{\bot}\subset\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}.

As in Section
2.5
, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and for an irreducible representation
V
of
K_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
,
let
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)
and
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{1},V)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D_{1},V)
be the unitary representations of
\widetilde{G}
and
\widetilde{G}_{1}

with the minimal
\widetilde{K}
-type
\chi^{-\lambda}
and with the minimal
\widetilde{K}_{1}
-type
\chi_{1}^{-\lambda}\otimes V
respectively, if they exist.

In the following we assume
(G,G_{1})
is an irreducible symmetric pair. Then
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is a direct sum of at most two simple Jordan triple systems.
Let
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}=:r
,
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=:r_{2}
, and define
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\{1,2\}
by
{\rm d}\chi|_{\mathfrak{k}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}}=\varepsilon_{j}{\rm d}\chi_{j}
, or equivalently, by

(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\varepsilon_{j}(x|y)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j}},\qquad j=1,2,\quad x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{j},\quad y\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{j}.

(2.15)

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple, we write
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}=:\mathfrak{p}_{12}^{+}
,
and let
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}=:r^{\prime}
,
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}=r^{\prime\prime}
. Now we consider the restriction of the representation
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
of
\widetilde{G}
to the subgroup
\widetilde{G}_{1}
.
If
\lambda
satisfies (
2.13
), then the unitary representation
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
exists,
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
is discretely decomposable,
and every
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-submodule in
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
contains a
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}
-null vector,
that is, has an intersection with
(\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}})^{\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}=\bigl(\chi_{1}^{-\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)\cap\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
.
Especially,
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}=\chi^{-\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
holds if
\lambda>\frac{d}{2}(r-1)
,
and
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
is holomorphic discrete if
\lambda>p-1
. For such
\lambda
, according to the decomposition (Theorem
2.2
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{simple},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}}\bigoplus_{\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)\boxtimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{non-simple},

the following holds.

Theorem 2.5

(Kobayashi,
[
26
, Theorems 8.3 and 8.4]
)
.

(1)

Suppose
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple. For
\lambda>\frac{d}{2}(r-1)
, the restriction of
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
to the subgroup
\widetilde{G}_{1}
is
decomposed into the Hilbert direct sum of irreducible representations as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}}\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{simple},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}}\,\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}}\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)\boxtimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)\bigr),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{non-simple}.

(2)

Suppose
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0}
with
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0}
simple of rank
r_{0}
, and
\sigma\colon(x,y)\mapsto(y,x)
, so that
(G,G_{1})
is of the form
(G_{0}\times G_{0},\Delta(G_{0}))
.
For
\lambda,\mu>\frac{d}{2}(r_{0}-1)
, the tensor product representation
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{0})\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\mathcal{H}_{\mu}(D_{0})
is decomposed under the diagonal subgroup
\Delta\bigl(\widetilde{G}_{0}\bigr)

into the Hilbert direct sum of irreducible representations as

\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{0})\mathbin{\hat{\otimes}}\mathcal{H}_{\mu}(D_{0})\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{0}}}\mathcal{H}_{\lambda+\mu}\bigl(D_{0},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{0}\bigr)\bigr).

In the following, suppose
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is simple. Similar results also hold for the tensor product case.
Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
be the
\widetilde{G}
-invariant inner product on
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
,
which is originally defined for
\lambda>p-1
by (
2.9
).
The purpose of this article is to study the above decomposition by observing the inner product

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x},

\displaystyle f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr),

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x_{11})g(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x},\qquad

\displaystyle f(x_{11})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr),\ g(x_{22})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr),

(2.16)

where
z\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
x=x_{1}+x_{2}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
or
x=x_{11}+x_{12}+x_{22}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{12}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
,
and the subscript
x
stands for the variable of integration.

In the author’s previous article
[
44
]
, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
are of tube type, we computed the above inner products explicitly
for some special
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
or
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
, that is, for

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}=(k+l,k,\dots,k),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{simple},\ \varepsilon_{2}=1,

\displaystyle\mathbf{k}=(k+1,\dots,k+1,k\dots,k),\qquad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{simple},\ \varepsilon_{2}=2,

\displaystyle(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})=((k,\dots,k),\mathbf{l}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{non-simple},

and applied these results for determination of the
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-intertwining operators (symmetry breaking operators)
