\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\mathbf{k}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{simple},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)\boxtimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)\bigr)\qquad

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\colon\ \text{non-simple}

for the above special
\mathbf{k}
or
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})
. In this article we treat general
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
or
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
,
compute the top term of (
2.16
), i.e., the value of (
2.16
) at
z_{1}=0
or
z_{12}=0
,
and also compute the poles of (
2.16
) as a function of
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

In the following, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple, we write
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}=(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
,
and write
\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr):=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)\boxtimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)
. Then since the map

\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr),\qquad f(x_{2})\longmapsto\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{z_{1}=0}

is
K_{1}
-equivariant and since
\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
decomposes multiplicity-freely under
K_{1}
by Theorem
2.2
,
there exist constants
C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{z_{1}=0}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)f(z_{2}),\qquad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)

(2.17)

holds for every
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
or
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then the Parseval–Plancherel-type formula is given by using these constants. Let
V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}
be an abstract
K_{1}
-module isomorphic to
\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
,
let
\|\cdot\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}
be the
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-invariant norm on
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)

normalized such that
\|v\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}=|v|_{V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}}
holds for all constant functions
v\in V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}
,
and let
\|\cdot\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
be the Fischer norm on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
given in (
2.10
).

Proposition 2.6
.

For
\lambda>p-1
and for
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}

(
when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is simple
)
or
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}

(
when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple
)
,
let
C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\in\mathbb{C}
be as in (
2.17
).

(1)

For
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
, we have
\|f\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\|f\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}
.

(2)

We take a vector-valued polynomial
\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2},\overline{V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}}\bigr)^{K_{1}}
normalized such that

\big|\langle f(x_{2}),\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\rangle_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\big|_{V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}}^{2}=\|f\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2},\qquad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr),

and define the vector-valued polynomial
F_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}(z)\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{-},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)
by

F_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}(z):=\frac{1}{C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)}\bigl\langle{\rm e}^{(x|z)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}},\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}.

(2.18)

Then the differential operator

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr),\qquad\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\bigr)(x_{1}):=F_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\right)f(x)\biggr|_{x_{2}=0}

(2.19)

becomes a symmetry breaking operator satisfying

\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}^{2}=\|f\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2},\qquad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr).

(3)

For
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}^{2}
.

Proof.

(1) Since the reproducing kernel of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
is given by
{\rm e}^{(z|\overline{x})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}
(see
[
13
, Proposition XI.1.1]
),
for
f,g\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
we have

\langle f(x),g(x)\rangle_{\lambda,x}=\bigl\langle f(x),\bigl\langle g(z),{\rm e}^{(z|\overline{x})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{F,z}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}=\bigl\langle\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x},g(z)\bigr\rangle_{F,z}.

Then by putting
f=g\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, we get the desired formula.

(2) The symmetry breaking property follows from
[
43
, Theorem 3.10 (1)]
. Let
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
.
Then since
\exp\bigl(x\big|\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}f(y)\big|_{y=0}=f(x)
holds, by (1), we have

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\bigr)(y_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)}\,\biggl\langle\exp\left(x\middle|\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\right)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}},\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\biggr\rangle_{\lambda,x}f(y_{2})\biggr|_{y_{2}=0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)}\left\langle f(x_{2}),\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\right\rangle_{\lambda,x}\biggr|_{y_{2}=0}=\left\langle f(x_{2}),\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\right\rangle_{F,x}\in V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}.

Then by the normalization of
\|\cdot\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}
and
\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})
, we get

\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}^{2}=\big|\langle f(x_{2}),\mathrm{K}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}(x_{2})\rangle_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\big|_{V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}}^{2}=\|f\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2},\qquad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr).

(3) Let

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}

be the
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-intertwining operator (holographic operator) normalized such that

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}\circ\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}=I_{\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)}

holds. Then since
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
decomposes multiplicity-freely under
\widetilde{G}_{1}
, for
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
, we have

f=\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f,\qquad\|f\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=\sum_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}.

If
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
, then since
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f=f
holds, by (1) and (2), we have

\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=\|f\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\|f\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}^{2},

and since
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}
is an isometry up to scalar multiple,

\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\uparrow}\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\big\|\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}f\big\|_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}}^{2}

holds for all
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
. Hence we get the desired formula.
∎

Next we consider the meromorphic continuation of (
2.16
), (
2.18
), and (
2.19
).
The differential operator
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
in (
2.19
) is extended to the map

\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr),

and this is meromorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.
For generic
\lambda
, we have an abstract
(\mathfrak{g}_{1},\widetilde{K}_{1})
-isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)_{\widetilde{K}_{1}}\simeq{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1}))\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})=\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}},

where
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1})
is the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}
, and
{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}
is the differential of
(\tau_{\lambda},\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D))
.
The restriction of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
gives the explicit isomorphism from the right to the left.
On the other hand, this does not hold for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.
By computing the poles of (
2.16
) with respect to
\lambda
, we can get some information on
{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1}))\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
for such singular
\lambda
.

Proposition 2.7
.

Let
\mathbf{l}=(l_{1},\dots,l_{r})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
. Suppose

(\lambda)_{\mathbf{l},d}\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

is holomorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
for some non-zero
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
.

(1)

We have

\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}\\
m_{j}\leq l_{j},\,j=1,\dots,r\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}),

and especially, for
j=1,\dots,r
,

{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1}))\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset M_{j}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)\qquad\text{holds if}\quad\lambda\in\frac{d}{2}(j-1)-l_{j}-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},

where
M_{j}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)\subset\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is as in (
2.12
).

(2)

For
a=0,1,\dots,r-1
, if
l_{a+1}=0
and
C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\frac{d}{2}a,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\neq 0
,
then
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
is holomorphic at
\lambda=\frac{d}{2}a
, and its restriction gives the symmetry breaking operator

\mathcal{F}_{\frac{d}{2}a,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\frac{d}{2}a}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr).

Proof.

(1) Follows from the last paragraph of Section
2.5
.

(2) The holomorphy of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
at
\lambda=\frac{d}{2}a
is clear from the assumption.
For the latter claim,
\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset M_{a+1}^{\mathfrak{g}}\bigl(\frac{d}{2}a\bigr)=\mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
holds by (1),
and the image of
\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
by
\mathcal{F}_{\frac{d}{2}a,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
is non-zero by the normalization assumption of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
.
Therefore, the image of
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}(D)
by
\mathcal{F}_{\frac{d}{2}a,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}^{\downarrow}
becomes a non-zero unitary submodule of
\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon_{1}\frac{d}{2}a}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)
,
which is unique and denoted by
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{1}\frac{d}{2}a}\bigl(D_{1},V_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigr)
.
∎

Since
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}
decomposes multiplicity-freely
[
26
, Theorem A]
, symmetry breaking operators on
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
are unique up to constant multiple
for all
\lambda
in (
2.13
). On the other hand, we do not know a priori whether symmetry breaking operators on
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)
are unique or not for
\lambda\leq\frac{d}{2}(r-1)
.
For non-unique case see
[
33
, Section 9]
or Section
8
of this paper.
It is known that symmetry breaking operators on
\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)
are always given by differential operators (localness theorem,
[
32
, Theorem 5.3]
),
and their symbols are characterized as polynomial solutions of certain differential equations (F-method,
[
33
, Theorem 3.1]
).
That is, for
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
let
\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}
be the vector-valued differential operator

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\colon\ \mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\otimes\mathfrak{p}^{\mp},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}f(z):=\sum_{\alpha,\beta}\frac{1}{2}Q\bigl(e^{\mp}_{\alpha},e^{\mp}_{\beta}\bigr)z\frac{\partial^{2}f}{\partial z_{\alpha}^{\pm}\partial z_{\beta}^{\pm}}(z)+\lambda\sum_{\alpha}e_{\alpha}^{\mp}\frac{\partial f}{\partial z_{\alpha}^{\pm}}(z),

where
\big\{e_{\alpha}^{\mp}\big\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}
is a basis of
\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}
, and
\big\{z_{\alpha}^{\pm}\big\}
is the coordinate of
\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}
dual to
\big\{e_{\alpha}^{\mp}\big\}

(
Bessel operator

[
9
]
and
[
13
, Section XV.2]
), let

\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\bigr)_{1}\colon\ \mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\otimes\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}_{1}

be the orthogonal projection of
\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}
onto
\mathfrak{p}^{\mp}_{1}
, and let

\operatorname{Sol}_{\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)}\bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\bigr)_{1}\bigr):=\big\{f(z)\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}\bigr)\ \big|\ \bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\bigr)_{1}f=0\big\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D),\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}(D_{1},V))

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathfrak{k}_{1}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}_{1}^{-}}\bigl(\chi_{1}^{\varepsilon_{1}\lambda}\otimes V^{\vee},\operatorname{ind}_{\mathfrak{k}^{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{-}}^{\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}}\bigl(\chi^{\lambda}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigl(\operatorname{Sol}_{\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-})}\bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{-}}\bigr)_{1}\bigr)\otimes V\bigr)^{K_{1}}.

(2.20)

By this F-method, we can prove the following, which gives an analogue of
[
35
, Corollary 12.8 (3)]
.

Proposition 2.8
.

Suppose
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}_{2}
are of tube type.

(1)

For
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, the following differential operator intertwines the
\widetilde{G}
-action

\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{-}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\right)^{a}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{\frac{n}{r}-a}(D)\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\frac{n}{r}+a}(D).

(See
[
2
, Theorem 6.4]
,
[
20
, Propositions 1.2 and 2.2]
,
[
59
, Lemma 7.1, formulas (8.6)–(8.8)]
.)

(2)

We fix
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}

\bigl(
when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is simple
\bigr)
or
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}

(
when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple
)
,
and let
\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
,
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Suppose that
(\lambda)_{\mathbf{l},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}
is holomorphic at
\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a
, and

\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-a}(\lambda)_{\mathbf{l},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})

holds for some non-zero
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
. Then there exists
C\in\mathbb{C}
such that

(\lambda)_{\mathbf{l},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a}=C\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{a}\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\frac{n}{r}+a,x}

holds for all
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
.

We note that
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}(x_{11})\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(x_{22})
holds when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple.

Proof.

(1) By the proof of
[
13
, Proposition XV.2.4]
, as a differential operator we have

\mathcal{B}_{\frac{n}{r}-a}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\pm}}(z)^{a}=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\pm}}(z)^{a}\mathcal{B}_{\frac{n}{r}+a}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}},\qquad a\in\mathbb{C},

(2.21)

and hence for
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, as a polynomial we have
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\pm}}(z)^{a}\in\operatorname{Sol}_{\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{\pm})}\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\frac{n}{r}-a}^{\mathfrak{p}^{\pm}}\bigr)
.
Now since
\mathbb{C}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{\pm}}(z)^{a}\simeq\chi^{\mp 2a}
holds as a
K
-module, by applying the F-method for
\sigma=I_{\mathfrak{g}^{\mathbb{C}}}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}
case, we get the intertwining property.

(2) As in
[
44
, formula (2.21)]
, we have
\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\bigr)_{1}\bigl\langle g(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}=0
for all
g(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
,
and by (
2.21
), we have

\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\frac{n}{r}+a}^{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\bigr)_{1}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-a}(\lambda)_{\mathbf{l},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a}=0.

Hence under the polynomiality assumption, we have

\displaystyle\bigl(f(x_{2})\mapsto\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-a}(\lambda)_{\mathbf{l},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\bigl(f(x_2)\mapsto}{}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{1}}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr),\operatorname{Sol}_{\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})}\bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{B}_{\frac{n}{r}+a}^{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\bigr)_{1}\bigr)\bigr)=\mathbb{C}\bigl(f(x_{2})\mapsto\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\frac{n}{r}+a,x}\bigr),

where the last equality holds since the space (
2.20
) is 1-dimensional for
\lambda>\frac{n}{r}-1=\frac{d}{2}(r-1)
, and this completes the proof.
∎

3
Key lemmas

In this section, we introduce some equations on inner products and results on finite-dimensional representations of
{\rm U}(s)
used later.

3.1
Reduction to smaller algebras

We consider a simple Jordan triple system
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
. We fix a tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
and let
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
denote the eigenspace of
D(e,\overline{e})
with the eigenvalue 2, as in (
2.2
).
This
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
becomes of tube type.
For
x\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, let
x^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
be the orthogonal projection. Also, let
D\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
D^{\prime}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
be the corresponding bounded symmetric domains,
and let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}
be the weighted Bergman inner products on
D
,
D^{\prime}
respectively,
as in (
2.9
).

Proposition 3.1
.

We fix a tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, and let
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
. Then for
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>p-1
, for
f(x^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime})\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, we have

\bigl\langle f(x^{\prime}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\bigl\langle f(x^{\prime}),{\rm e}^{(x^{\prime}|\overline{z^{\prime}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x^{\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}.

Here the subscripts
x
,
x^{\prime}
denote the variables of integration. Note that
\bigl(x^{\prime}|\overline{z^{\prime}}\bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\bigl(x^{\prime}|\overline{z^{\prime}}\bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}
holds for any
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
,
since a primitive tripotent
\tilde{e}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
is also primitive in
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, and both inner products are normalized such that

\bigl(\tilde{e}|\overline{\tilde{e}}\bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\bigl(\tilde{e}|\overline{\tilde{e}}\bigr)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}=1
holds.
This lemma is proved easily by using Corollary
2.4
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
.
Here we give a proof that does not rely on Corollary
2.4
.

Proof.

We write
x=x_{2}+x_{1}+x_{0}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{0}
, so that
x^{\prime}=x_{2}
. First we prove

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x_{2}|\overline{z_{2}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

(3.1)

for
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\bigr)
. Since

{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}={\rm e}^{(x_{2}|\overline{z_{2}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{0}=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k_{1}!k_{0}!}(x_{1}|\overline{z_{1}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{k_{1}}(x_{0}|\overline{z_{0}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{k_{0}}

holds, it is enough to prove
\langle f(x_{2}),g(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=0
for
g(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})\in\mathcal{O}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{1}\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{0}\bigr)
satisfying

g(x_{2},sx_{1},tx_{0})=s^{k_{1}}t^{k_{0}}g(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0}),\qquad s,t\in\mathbb{C}

for some
k_{1},k_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
(k_{1},k_{0})\neq(0,0)
. For
t\in\sqrt{-1}\mathbb{R}
, let

l:=\exp(t(2I_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}-D(e,\overline{e})))=I_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}}+e^{t}I_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{1}}+{\rm e}^{2t}I_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{0}}\in K,

and let
\operatorname{Proj}_{2}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{+}\to\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
be the orthogonal projection. Then we have

\displaystyle\langle f(x_{2}),g(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\langle f\bigl(\operatorname{Proj}_{2}\bigl(l^{-1}(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})\bigr)\bigr),g(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

\displaystyle=\langle f(\operatorname{Proj}_{2}(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})),g(l(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0}))\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),g\bigl(x_{2},e^{t}x_{1},{\rm e}^{2t}x_{0}\bigr)\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

\displaystyle=\overline{{\rm e}^{(k_{1}+2k_{0})t}}\langle f(x_{2}),g(x_{2},x_{1},x_{0})\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

for all
t\in\sqrt{-1}\mathbb{R}
, and hence this vanishes if
(k_{1},k_{0})\neq(0,0)
. Therefore, (
3.1
) holds.
Next, to prove

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x_{2}|\overline{z_{2}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x_{2}|\overline{z_{2}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x_{2},\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}},

(3.2)

we check that the natural inclusion
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D^{\prime})_{\widetilde{K}(e)_{2}}=\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\bigr)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}=\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
intertwines the
\bigl(\mathfrak{g}(e)_{2},\widetilde{K}(e)_{2}\bigr)
-action,
where
\mathfrak{g}(e)_{2}
,
K(e)_{2}
are as in the last paragraph of Section
2.2
.
For example, by (
2.7
), the action of
\mathfrak{p}^{-}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}=\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
is given by

({\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(0,0,w)f)(x)=\lambda(x|w)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}f(x)+\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\biggr|_{t=0}f(x+tQ(x)w),\qquad w\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}.

If
w=w_{2}\in\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle(x|w_{2})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=(x_{2}|w_{2})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}},

\displaystyle x+tQ(x)w_{2}=(x_{2}+tQ(x_{2})w_{2})+(x_{1}+tQ(x_{2},x_{1})w_{2})+(x_{0}+tQ(x_{1})w_{2})

\displaystyle\hphantom{x+tQ(x)w_2=}{}\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{0},

and hence if
f(x)=f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\bigr)
, we have

({\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(0,0,w_{2})f)(x)=\lambda(x_{2}|w_{2})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}f(x_{2})+\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\biggr|_{t=0}f(x_{2}+tQ(x_{2})w_{2}).

Thus, the natural inclusion is
\mathfrak{p}^{-}(e)_{2}
-equivariant. The
\widetilde{K}(e)_{2}
- and
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
-equivariance are also proved similarly.
Hence, this is
\bigl(\mathfrak{g}(e)_{2},\widetilde{K}(e)_{2}\bigr)
-equivariant, and therefore is an isometry up to scalar multiple.
Moreover, by the normalization assumption
\|1\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}=\|1\|_{\lambda,\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}}=1
, this is exactly an isometry.
Hence (
3.2
) holds, and this proves the proposition.
∎

Now we consider an involution
\sigma
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, and let
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}:=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{\sigma}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}:=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}
,
K_{1}:=K^{\sigma}
as in Section
2.7
.
Then by Proposition
3.1
, the computation of

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x},\qquad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)

is reduced to the cases that both
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
are of tube type.
We take a maximal tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
of
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
, and let
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}(e)_{2}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
,

\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{1}:=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
. If

\bigl\langle f\bigl(x_{2}^{\prime}\bigr),{\rm e}^{(x^{\prime}|\overline{z^{\prime}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x^{\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}\big|_{z^{\prime}_{1}=0}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)f\bigl(z_{2}^{\prime}\bigr),\qquad f\bigl(x_{2}^{\prime}\bigr)\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}\bigr)

holds for some
C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\in\mathbb{C}
, then by Proposition
3.1
,

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\big|_{z_{1}=0}=C_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}}\bigl(\lambda,\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)f(z_{2})

holds for all
f(x_{2})=f\bigl(x_{2}^{\prime}\bigr)\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
, and by the
K_{1}
-equivariance,
this also holds for all
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
.
Similarly, let
b(\lambda)\in\mathbb{C}[\lambda]
. If for all
f\bigl(x_{2}^{\prime}\bigr)\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}\bigr)
,

b(\lambda)\bigl\langle f\bigl(x_{2}^{\prime}\bigr),{\rm e}^{(x^{\prime}|\overline{z^{\prime}})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x^{\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}}

is holomorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
, then for all
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
,

b(\lambda)\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}

is also holomorphically continued for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
. That is, these computation for
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
is reduced to those for
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime},\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}\bigr)
,
and both
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+\prime}_{2}
are of tube type, with a common maximal tripotent
e
.

3.2
Rodrigues-type formulas

In this subsection let
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
be simple and of tube type. Let
r:=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
n:=\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}=r+\frac{d}{2}r(r-1)
as in (
2.4
).
In this case
p=\frac{2n}{r}
holds. We fix a maximal tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, let
\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
be the corresponding symmetric cone and the Euclidean real form,
and let
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)
be the determinant polynomial. We normalize the differential operator
\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
with respect to the bilinear form

(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}=(\cdot|Q(\overline{e})\cdot)_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}
. Also, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we write

\underline{k}_{r}:=(\underbrace{k,\dots,k}_{r})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r},

and for
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
,
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
, let
(\lambda)_{\mathbf{m},d}
be as in (
2.11
).
First we recall the following theorem from the author’s previous article
[
44
]
.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>\frac{2n}{r}-1
,
z,a\in\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
.

(1)

For
f\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\frac{\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{n}}\int_{a+\sqrt{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{+}}{\rm e}^{(z|w)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}}f\bigl(w^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(w)^{-\lambda}\,{\rm d}w,

where
\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda):=(2\pi)^{dr(r-1)/4}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\Gamma\bigl(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(j-1)\bigr)
.

(See
[
44
, Corollary 4.3]
.)

(2)

Let
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
. We take
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

f_{1}^{\sharp(k)}(x):=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{k}f_{1}\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)

is a polynomial. Then we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f_{1}(x)f_{2}(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}f_{1}^{\sharp(k)}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\right)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+k-\frac{n}{r}}\bigl\langle f_{2}(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda+k,x}.

(See
[
44
, Theorem 4.1]
.)

Next we consider an involution
\sigma
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, let
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}:=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{\sigma}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}:=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}
, and assume
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is also of tube type.
We take a common maximal tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
of
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, and let
\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
be the corresponding Euclidean real forms.
When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple, then we also write
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{12}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
,
\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}=:\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}
.
Then as in the proof of
[
44
, Theorem 4.4]
, when
f_{1}(x)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{k}
, we have

\bigl(\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}^{k}\bigr)^{\sharp(2k/\varepsilon_{2})}(x)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{2k/\varepsilon_{2}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\bigl(\operatorname{Proj}_{2}\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\bigr)^{k}=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{k},

where
\varepsilon_{2}
is as in (
2.15
), and
\operatorname{Proj}_{2}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{+}\to\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
denotes the orthogonal projection.
Similarly, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
and
f_{1}(x)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}(x_{11})^{k}
or
f_{1}(x)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(x_{22})^{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl(\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}^{k}\bigr)^{\sharp(k)}(x)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}\bigl(\operatorname{Proj}_{11}\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\bigr)^{k}=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(x_{22})^{k},

\displaystyle\bigl(\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}^{k}\bigr)^{\sharp(k)}(x)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}\bigl(\operatorname{Proj}_{22}\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\bigr)^{k}=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}(x_{11})^{k},

where
\operatorname{Proj}_{jj}\colon\mathfrak{p}^{+}\to\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{jj}
denotes the orthogonal projection.
Thus the following holds. Here we normalize the differential operator
\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
with respect to the bilinear form

(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}=\varepsilon_{2}^{-1}(\cdot|\cdot)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}
on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=\mathfrak{n}^{+\mathbb{C}}_{2}
.

Theorem 3.3
.

(1)

Let
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>\frac{2n}{r}-1
and let
f\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
. Then for
z=z_{1}+z_{2}\in\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{k}f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{2k/\varepsilon_{2}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\!\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\right)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+\frac{2k}{\varepsilon_{2}}-\frac{n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda+\frac{2k}{\varepsilon_{2}},x}.

(2)

Suppose
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
, let
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>\frac{2n}{r}-1
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}

and let
f\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
. Then for
z=z_{11}+z_{12}+z_{22}\in\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}(x_{11})^{k}f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{22}}\right)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+k-\frac{n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda+k,x},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(x_{22})^{k}f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{11}}\right)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+k-\frac{n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda+k,x}.

Similarly, we consider the outer tensor product
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)\mathbin{\hat{\boxtimes}}\mathcal{H}_{\mu}(D)
of Hilbert spaces, and let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu}
denote its inner product.
Then the following holds.

Theorem 3.4
.

Let
\operatorname{Re}\lambda,\operatorname{Re}\mu>\frac{2n}{r}-1
and let
f(x,y)\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}\bigr)
. Then for
z,-w\in\Omega\subset\mathfrak{n}^{+}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x,y)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x-y)^{k},{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}+(y|\overline{w})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu,(x,y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}(\mu)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(-w)^{-\mu+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}-\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}\times\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+k-\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(-w)^{\mu+k-\frac{n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x,y),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}+(y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{(\lambda+k)\otimes(\mu+k),(x,y)},

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x,y)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x-y)^{k},{\rm e}^{(x-y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu,(x,y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda-\mu+\frac{2n}{r}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}(\mu)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\!\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\right)\!\!{\vphantom{\biggr)}}^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+\mu+2k-\frac{2n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x,y),{\rm e}^{(x-y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{(\lambda+k)\otimes(\mu+k),(x,y)}.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.2
(1), for
a,z,-w\in\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x,y)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x-y)^{k},{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}+(y|\overline{w})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu,(x,y)}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\bigl\langle f(x,-y)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x+y)^{k},{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}-(y|\overline{w})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu,(x,y)}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(-w)^{-\mu+\frac{n}{r}}\frac{\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda)\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\mu)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{2n}}\iint_{(a+\sqrt{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{+})^{2}}{\rm e}^{(x|z)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}-(y|w)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}\times f\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}},-y^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}}+y^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{-\lambda}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(y)^{-\mu}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}y,

and since

\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}}+y^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x+y)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{-1}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(y)^{-1}

holds (see
[
13
, Lemma X.4.4 (i)]
), by putting
w=-z
, we get

\displaystyle\bigl\langle f(x,y)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x-y)^{k},{\rm e}^{(x-y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu,(x,y)}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda-\mu+\frac{2n}{r}}\frac{\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda)\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\mu)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{2n}}\iint_{(a+\sqrt{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{+})^{2}}{\rm e}^{(x+y|z)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times f\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}},-y^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x+y)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{-\lambda-k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(y)^{-\mu-k}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda-\mu+\frac{2n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\right)^{k}\frac{\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda)\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\mu)}{(2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{2n}}\iint_{(a+\sqrt{-1}\mathfrak{n}^{+})^{2}}{\rm e}^{(x+y|z)_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times f\bigl(x^{\mathit{-1}},-y^{\mathit{-1}}\bigr)\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(x)^{-\lambda-k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(y)^{-\mu-k}\,{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda-\mu+\frac{2n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\right)^{k}\frac{\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda)\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\mu)}{\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\lambda+k)\Gamma_{r}^{d}(\mu+k)}

\displaystyle\quad{}\times\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{(\lambda+k)+(\mu+k)-\frac{2n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x,-y),{\rm e}^{(x+y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{(\lambda+k)\otimes(\mu+k),(x,y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda-\mu+\frac{2n}{r}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}(\mu)_{\underline{k}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\!\!\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\right)^{k}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+\mu+2k-\frac{2n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x,y),{\rm e}^{(x-y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{(\lambda+k)\otimes(\mu+k),(x,y)}.

This proves the 2nd formula. The 1st formula is also proved similarly.
∎

These theorems give analogues of the Rodrigues formulas for Jacobi polynomials.
Especially, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is simple, let
r_{2}:=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
, fix a Jordan frame
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{r_{2}}\}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
,
and for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
, let
\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
be as in (
2.5
).
Then by putting
k=k_{r_{2}}
,
f(x)=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r_{2}}}_{r_{2}}}(x_{2})
in Theorem
3.3
(1), we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}=\Bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{k_{r_{2}}}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r_{2}}}_{r_{2}}}(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\Bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

(3.3)

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{2k_{r_{2}}/\varepsilon_{2}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\right)^{k_{r_{2}}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+\frac{2k_{r_{2}}}{\varepsilon_{2}}-\frac{n}{r}}\Bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r_{2}}}_{r_{2}}}(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\Bigr\rangle_{\lambda+\frac{2k_{r_{2}}}{\varepsilon_{2}},x}.

Then
\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r_{2}}}_{r_{2}}}(x_{2})
depends only on the smaller algebra
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e^{\prime})_{2}
, with
e^{\prime}=e_{1}+\cdots+e_{r_{2}-1}
,
and by Proposition
3.1
, computation of the inner product on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is reduced to that on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e^{\prime})_{2}
.
Similarly, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
is not simple, let
r^{\prime}:=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}
,
r^{\prime\prime}:=\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
, fix Jordan frames in
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
,
and for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
, let
\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{11})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)
,
\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)
be as in
(
2.5
). Then by putting
k=k_{r^{\prime}}
,
f(x)=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r^{\prime}}}(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22})

or
k=l_{r^{\prime\prime}}
,
f(x)=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}-\underline{l_{r^{\prime\prime}}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}(x_{22})
in Theorem
3.3
(2), we have

\displaystyle\Bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\Bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{22}}\right)^{k_{r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\ {}\times\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{n}{r}}\Bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r^{\prime}}}\!(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\Bigr\rangle_{\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}},x}

(3.4)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{l_{r^{\prime\prime}}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{11}}\right)^{l_{r^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\ {}\times\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+l_{r^{\prime\prime}}-\frac{n}{r}}\Bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}-\underline{l_{r^{\prime\prime}}}_{r^{\prime\prime}}}(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\Bigr\rangle_{\lambda+l_{r^{\prime\prime}},x},

(3.5)

and again by Proposition
3.1
, computation of the inner product on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is reduced to that on a smaller algebra of rank
r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}-1
.
Similarly, for
\mathfrak{p}^{+}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}
case, for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
, by putting
k=k_{r}
,
f(x,y)=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r}}_{r}}(x-y)
in Theorem
3.4
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x-y),{\rm e}^{(x-y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda\otimes\mu,(x,y)}

(3.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\hskip-1.0pt(z)^{-\lambda-\mu+\frac{2n}{r}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k_{r}}_{r},d}(\mu)_{\underline{k_{r}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\!\!\left(\hskip-1.0pt\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\hskip-1.0pt\right)\!\!{\vphantom{\biggr|}}^{k_{r}}\!\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}\hskip-1.0pt(z)^{\lambda+\mu+2k_{r}-\frac{2n}{r}}\hskip-1.0pt\Big\langle\hskip-1.0pt\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r}}_{r}}\!(x\hskip-1.0pt-\hskip-1.0pty),{\rm e}^{(x-y|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\hskip-1.0pt\Big\rangle_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\lambda+k_{r})\otimes\hskip 14.0pt\\
(\mu+k_{r}),(x,y)\end{subarray}}\!\!,

and again by Proposition
3.1
, computation of the inner product on
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is reduced to that on a smaller algebra of rank
r-1
.
For
\mathbf{k}=\underline{k}_{r}
case, see also
[
6
,
7
]
.

In addition, by Proposition
2.8
(2) and Theorem
3.3
, we easily get the following, which is an analogue of
[
35
, Corollary 12.8 (3)]
.
We note that
\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}(x_{11})\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(x_{22})
holds, when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple.

Theorem 3.5
.

We fix
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}

(
when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is simple
)
or
\tilde{\mathbf{k}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}

(
when
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is not simple
)
, and
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Then
(\lambda)_{\underline{2a}_{r},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}
is
holomorphically continued for
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>\frac{n}{r}-2a-1
, and there exist
C_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}},a}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+},\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\displaystyle(\lambda)_{\underline{2a}_{r},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a}=C_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}},a}^{\mathfrak{n}^{+},\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{a}\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\frac{n}{r}+a,x}

holds for all
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
.

Proof.

Since
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}
is holomorphically continued for
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>\frac{n}{r}-1=\frac{d}{2}(r-1)
, by Theorem
3.3
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda-\frac{n}{r}}(\lambda)_{\underline{2a}_{r},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{}=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\right)^{\varepsilon_{2}a}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{\lambda+2a-\frac{n}{r}}\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda+2a,x}

is holomorphically continued for
\operatorname{Re}\lambda+2a>\frac{n}{r}-1
, and when
\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{-a}(\lambda)_{\underline{2a}_{r},d}\bigl\langle\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}(x_{2})^{\varepsilon_{2}a}f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{\lambda=\frac{n}{r}-a}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{2}}\right)^{\varepsilon_{2}a}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z)^{a}\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\frac{n}{r}+a,x}

becomes a polynomial. Hence the theorem follows from Proposition
2.8
(2).
∎

3.3
Tensor product of finite-dimensional representations of
\boldsymbol{{\rm U}(s)}

In this subsection we treat some results on finite-dimensional representations of
{\rm U}(s)
used later, which easily follows from the Littlewood–Richardson rule.
As in Section
2.6
, for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
, let
V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}
be the irreducible representation of
{\rm U}(s)
with the highest weight
\mathbf{k}
,
and we write
\mathbf{k}^{\vee}:=(k_{s},\dots,k_{1})
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l},\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
. If
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{n}}^{(s)},V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}\otimes V_{\mathbf{l}}^{(s)}\bigr)\neq\{0\}
, then we have

(1)

n_{s-i-j}\geq k_{s-i}+l_{s-j}
for
0\leq i,j<s
,
i+j<s
.

(2)

n_{i+j-1}\leq k_{i}+l_{j}
for
1\leq i,j\leq s
,
i+j\leq s+1
.

Proof.

(1) Suppose
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{n}}^{(s)},V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}\otimes V_{\mathbf{l}}^{(s)}\bigr)\neq\{0\}
. Then by the Littlewood–Richardson rule,
there exists a skew semistandard tableau
Y
of shape
\mathbf{n}/\mathbf{k}
, weight
\mathbf{l}
with the lattice word condition.
Let
Y(a,b)

(1\leq a\leq s,\,k_{a}+1\leq b\leq n_{a})
be the
(a,b)
-entry of
Y
, so that
{\#\{(a,b)\mid Y(a,b)=j\}=l_{j}}
holds.
Then by the lattice word condition, we have

\displaystyle n_{s-i-j}-k_{s-i}

\displaystyle\geq\#\{(a,b)\mid Y(a,b)=s-i-j,\,a\leq s-i\}

\displaystyle\geq\#\{(a,b)\mid Y(a,b)=s-i-j+1,\,a\leq s-i+1\}

\displaystyle\geq\#\{(a,b)\mid Y(a,b)=s-i-j+2,\,a\leq s-i+2\}\geq\cdots

\displaystyle\geq\#\{(a,b)\mid Y(a,b)=s-j,\,a\leq s\}=l_{s-j}.

(2) Since
\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{n}}^{(s)},V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}\otimes V_{\mathbf{l}}^{(s)}\bigr)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\underline{k_{1}+l_{1}}_{s}-\mathbf{n}^{\vee}}^{(s)},V_{\underline{k_{1}}_{s}-\mathbf{k}^{\vee}}^{(s)}\otimes V_{\underline{l_{1}}_{s}-\mathbf{l}^{\vee}}^{(s)}\bigr)
holds,
by (1) we have
k_{1}+l_{1}-n_{i+j+1}\geq(k_{1}-k_{i+1})+(l_{1}-l_{j+1})
, that is,
n_{i+j+1}\leq k_{i+1}+l_{j+1}
holds.
∎

Using this lemma, we give necessary conditions on
\bigl(\tilde{\mathbf{k}},\mathbf{m}\bigr)
for existence of non-zero
K_{1}
-homomorphisms
from
\mathcal{P}_{\tilde{\mathbf{k}}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
to
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, when
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
are of tube type and
K_{1}
is of type
A
.
First we consider the cases
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)=({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Sym}(s,\mathbb{C}))
,
({\rm M}(2s,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(2s,\mathbb{C}))
.
Then for
(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
or
\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{2s}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
, by (
2.14
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{n}}(\operatorname{Sym}(s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C})))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{2\mathbf{n}}^{(s)},V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)}\otimes V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)}\bigr),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(2s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{n}}(\operatorname{Alt}(2s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm M}(2s,\mathbb{C})))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(2s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{n}^{2}}^{(2s)},V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(2s)}\otimes V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(2s)}\bigr).

Hence by Lemma
3.6
(1),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{n}}(\operatorname{Sym}(s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C})))\neq\{0\}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{implies}\ m_{s-i}+m_{s-j}\leq 2n_{s-i-j},

(3.7)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(2s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{n}}(\operatorname{Alt}(2s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm M}(2s,\mathbb{C})))\neq\{0\}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{implies}\ m_{2s-i}+m_{2s-j}\leq n_{\lceil(2s-i-j)/2\rceil}.

(3.8)

Next we consider the cases
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)=(\operatorname{Sym}(2s,\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C}))
,
(\operatorname{Alt}(2s,\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C}))
.
In general, for
\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l},\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
, if
s=s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}
,
k_{s^{\prime}+1}=\cdots=k_{s}=0
and
l_{s^{\prime\prime}+1}=\cdots=l_{s}=0
, then by
[
15
, Theorem 9.2.3]
, we have

\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)},V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}\otimes V_{\mathbf{l}}^{(s)}\bigr)=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s^{\prime})\times{\rm U}(s^{\prime\prime})}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s^{\prime})}\boxtimes V_{\mathbf{l}}^{(s^{\prime\prime})},V_{\mathbf{m}}^{(s)}\bigr).

Especially, for
(\mathbf{m},\mathbf{k})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{2s}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
or
\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}\times\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{s}
, since

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)\times{\rm U}(s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\operatorname{Sym}(2s,\mathbb{C})))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)\times{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}\boxtimes V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)},V_{2\mathbf{m}}^{(2s)}\bigr),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)\times{\rm U}(s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\operatorname{Alt}(2s,\mathbb{C})))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)\times{\rm U}(s)}\bigl(V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)}\boxtimes V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(s)},V_{\mathbf{m}^{2}}^{(2s)}\bigr)

hold by (
2.14
), by Lemma
3.6
(2),

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)\times{\rm U}(s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\operatorname{Sym}(2s,\mathbb{C})))\neq\{0\}\quad\ \,\text{implies}\ 2m_{i+j-1}\leq k_{i}+k_{j},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{{\rm U}(s)\times{\rm U}(s)}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}({\rm M}(s,\mathbb{C})),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\operatorname{Alt}(2s,\mathbb{C})))\neq\{0\}\quad\quad\text{implies}\ m_{\lfloor(i+j)/2\rfloor}\leq k_{i}+k_{j}

(3.10)

for
i+j\leq 2s+1
, where we set
k_{s+1}=\cdots=k_{2s}=0
. Similarly, for the cases

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22},K_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Sym}(r^{\prime},\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Sym}(r^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{C}),{\rm U}(r^{\prime})\times{\rm U}(r^{\prime\prime})),\\
({\rm M}(r,\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(r^{\prime},\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(r^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{C}),{\rm U}(r^{\prime})\times{\rm U}(r^{\prime})\times{\rm U}(r^{\prime\prime})\times{\rm U}(r^{\prime\prime})),\\
(\operatorname{Alt}(2r,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(2r^{\prime},\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(2r^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{C}),{\rm U}(2r^{\prime})\times{\rm U}(2r^{\prime\prime})),\end{cases}

by Lemma
3.6
(2), for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
,
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{1}}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)\boxtimes\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr),\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})\bigr)\neq\{0\}\qquad\text{implies}\quad m_{i+j-1}\leq k_{i}+l_{j}.

(3.11)

4
Easy case

In this section we treat
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{\sigma}\oplus(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
such that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}(e)_{2}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}
holds for some (or equivalently any)
maximal tripotent
e\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
, where
\mathfrak{p}^{+}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}(e)_{2}\subset\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
are as in (
2.2
).
This section contains some overlap with
[
44
, Sections 6.1 and 7]
, but for the sake of completeness, we give the results for this case.
Such
\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
are one of

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)=\begin{cases}({\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(q,s^{\prime};\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(q,s^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }1),\\
(\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(s-1,\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(s-1,1;\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }2),\\
(\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(s-1,1;\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(s-1,\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }3),\\
\bigl(\mathbb{C}^{2s},\bigl(\bigl(1,\sqrt{-1}\bigr)\mathbb{C}\bigr)^{s},\bigl(\bigl(1,-\sqrt{-1}\bigr)\mathbb{C}\bigr)^{s}\bigr)\\
\quad{}\simeq\bigl(\mathbb{C}^{2s},{\rm M}(1,s;\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(1,s;\mathbb{C})\bigr)&(\text{Case }4),\\
\bigl({\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{O}^{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{O}^{\mathbb{C}}\bigr)\simeq\bigl({\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{C}^{8},\mathbb{C}^{8}\bigr)&(\text{Case }5)\end{cases}

(
s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}=s
for Case 1). Then the corresponding symmetric pairs are

(G,G_{1})=\begin{cases}({\rm SU}(q,s),{\rm S}({\rm U}(q,s^{\prime})\times{\rm U}(s^{\prime\prime})))&(\text{Case 1}),\\
({\rm SO}^{*}(2s),{\rm SO}^{*}(2(s-1))\times{\rm SO}(2))&(\text{Case 2}),\\
({\rm SO}^{*}(2s),{\rm U}(s-1,1))&(\text{Case 3}),\\
({\rm SO}_{0}(2,2s),{\rm U}(1,s))&(\text{Case 4}),\\
(E_{6(-14)},{\rm U}(1)\times{\rm Spin}_{0}(2,8))&(\text{Case 5}).\end{cases}

Let
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}=:r
,
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=:r_{2}
, and let
d
be the number defined in (
2.4
), so that

(r,r_{2},d)=\begin{cases}(\min\{q,s\},\min\{q,s^{\prime\prime}\},2)&(\text{Case }1),\\
(\lfloor s/2\rfloor,1,4)&(\text{Case }2),\\
(\lfloor s/2\rfloor,\lfloor(s-1)/2\rfloor,4)&(\text{Case }3),\\
(2,1,2s-2)&(\text{Case }4),\\
(2,2,6)&(\text{Case }5).\end{cases}

For these cases, for
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
, since
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
are generated by
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}\hskip-1.0pt(e)_{2}\bigr)=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\hskip-1.0pt(e)_{2}\bigr)

as
K^{\mathbb{C}}
- and
K^{\mathbb{C}}_{1}
-modules respectively, we have
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+})
, and hence by Corollary
2.4
and Theorem
2.3
, the following holds.
Here
(\lambda)_{\mathbf{k},d}
is as in (
2.11
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>p-1
,
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
, and let
f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
. Then we have

\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\mathbf{k},d}}f(z_{2}),\qquad\|f(x_{2})\|_{\lambda,x}^{2}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\mathbf{k},d}}\|f(z_{2})\|_{F,z}^{2}.

Next we consider the decomposition of the holomorphic discrete series representation of scalar type
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
under the subgroup
\widetilde{G}_{1}\subset\widetilde{G}
.
By Theorem
2.5
, we have

\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}}\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr))\simeq\begin{cases}\mathcal{H}_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\bigl(D_{{\rm U}(q,s^{\prime})},V_{\mathbf{k}}^{(q)\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}\bigr)\boxtimes V_{(\lambda_{2},\dots,\lambda_{2})+\mathbf{k}}^{(s^{\prime\prime})}&(\text{Case }1),\\
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}\bigl(D_{{\rm SO}^{*}(2(s-1))},V_{(k,0,\dots,0)}^{(s-1)\vee}\bigr)\boxtimes\chi_{{\rm SO}(2)}^{-\lambda-2k}&(\text{Case }2),\\
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{\lambda}{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}}\bigl(D_{{\rm U}(s-1,1)},V_{\mathbf{k}^{2}}^{(s-1)\vee}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}\bigr)&(\text{Case }3),\\
\mathcal{H}_{(\lambda+k)+0}\bigl(D_{{\rm U}(1,s)},\mathbb{C}\boxtimes V_{(0,\dots,0,-k)}^{(s)}\bigr)&(\text{Case }4),\\
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda+\frac{|\mathbf{k}|}{2}}\bigl(D_{{\rm Spin}_{0}(2,8)},V_{\!\!\left(\frac{k_{1}+k_{2}}{2},\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{2},\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{2},-\frac{k_{1}-k_{2}}{2}\right)}^{[8]\vee}\bigr)\!\boxtimes\!\chi_{{\rm U}(1)}^{-\lambda-\frac{3}{2}|\mathbf{k}|}\!\!&(\text{Case }5),\end{cases}

where we set
\lambda=\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}
for Case 1.
For each
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
, let
V_{\mathbf{k}}
be an abstract
K_{1}
-module isomorphic to
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)
,
and take non-zero
K_{1}
-invariant vector-valued polynomials
{\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\vee}(z_{2})\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{2},V_{\mathbf{k}})^{K_{1}}}
and
\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2},\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(V_{\mathbf{k}},\mathbb{C})\bigr)^{K_{1}}
.
Such polynomials are unique up to constant multiple. Then by
[
43
, Section 5.1]
,
[
33
, Section 5]
, the normal derivative and the multiplication operators

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\downarrow}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D_{1},V_{\mathbf{k}}),\qquad

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\downarrow}f)(x_{1}):=\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\vee}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}\right)f(x)\biggr|_{x_{2}=0},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\uparrow}\colon\ \mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D_{1},V_{\mathbf{k}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}},\qquad

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\uparrow}g)(x):=\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{2})g(x_{1})

give the
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-intertwining operators (symmetry breaking operator, holographic operator) for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\|\cdot\|_{\lambda,\mathbf{k}}
be the
\widetilde{G}_{1}
-invariant norm on
\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D_{1},V_{\mathbf{k}})

normalized such that
\|v\|_{\lambda,\mathbf{k}}=|v|_{V_{\mathbf{k}}}
holds for all constant functions
v\in V_{\mathbf{k}}
.
Then by Proposition
2.6
(3), the following Parseval–Plancherel-type formula holds.

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\lambda>p-1
. For each
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
, we take a vector-valued polynomial
{\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\vee}(z_{2})\in\mathcal{P}(\mathfrak{p}^{-}_{2},V_{\mathbf{k}})^{K_{1}}}
normalized such that

\big|\bigl\langle f(x_{2}),\overline{\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\vee}(\overline{x_{2}})}\bigr\rangle_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}\big|_{V_{\mathbf{k}}}^{2}=\|f\|_{F,\mathfrak{p}^{+}}^{2},\qquad f(x_{2})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr).

Then for
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\lambda}(D)
, we have

\|f\|_{\lambda}^{2}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}}\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\mathbf{k},d}}\biggl\|\mathrm{K}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\vee}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{2}}\right)f(x)\biggr|_{x_{2}=0}\biggr\|_{\lambda,\mathbf{k},x_{1}}^{2}.

Also, by Proposition
2.7
, we have the following.

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}
, and set
k_{r_{2}+1}=\cdots=k_{r}:=0
. Then for
a=1,2,\dots,r
,

{\rm d}\tau_{\lambda}(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}_{1}))\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\subset M_{a}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)

holds if and only if

\lambda\in\frac{d}{2}(a-1)-k_{a}-\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

Especially, for
a=0,1,\dots,r-1
, we have

\mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}(D)|_{\widetilde{G}_{1}}\simeq\sideset{}{{}^{\oplus}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r_{2}}\\
k_{a+1}=0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}\bigl(D_{1},\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}\bigr)\bigr).

Here
M_{a}^{\mathfrak{g}}(\lambda)\!\subset\!\mathcal{O}_{\lambda}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}
is the
\bigl(\mathfrak{g},\widetilde{K}\bigr)
-submodule given in (
2.12
),
so that
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}(D)_{\widetilde{K}}\!\simeq\!M_{a+1}^{\mathfrak{g}}\hskip-1.0pt\bigl(\frac{d}{2}a\bigr)
holds for
a=0,1,\dots,r-1
.
The decomposition of
\mathcal{H}_{\frac{d}{2}a}(D)
for
a=1
is earlier given in
[
41
]
,
and we can also prove those for
a=2,\dots,r-1
for Cases 1–3 by using the seesaw dual pair theory (see, e.g.,
[
18
, Section 3]
,
[
37
]
) as in
[
41
]
.

5
Case
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}}
is non-simple

In this section we treat
\mathfrak{p}^{+}=(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{\sigma}\oplus(\mathfrak{p}^{+})^{-\sigma}=\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
such that
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}
is non-simple,
and write
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{2}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\oplus\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{1}=:\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{12}
, so that

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{12},\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(\mathbb{C}^{d+2},\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{d},\mathbb{C})&(\text{Case }1),\\
(\operatorname{Sym}(r,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Sym}(r^{\prime},\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Sym}(r^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }2),\\
({\rm M}(q,s;\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(q^{\prime},s^{\prime};\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(q^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})\oplus{\rm M}(q^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime};\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(q^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }3),\\
(\operatorname{Alt}(s,\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(s^{\prime},\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Alt}(s^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }4),\\
(\operatorname{Herm}(3,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{C},{\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\operatorname{Herm}(2,\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}})&(\text{Case }5),\\
({\rm M}(1,2;\mathbb{O})^{\mathbb{C}},\mathbb{C},\operatorname{Alt}(5,\mathbb{C}),{\rm M}(1,5;\mathbb{C}))&(\text{Case }6)\end{cases}

with
r=r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}
,
q=q^{\prime}+q^{\prime\prime}
,
s=s^{\prime}+s^{\prime\prime}
. Then the corresponding symmetric pairs are

(G,G_{1})=\begin{cases}({\rm SO}_{0}(2,d+2),{\rm SO}_{0}(2,d)\times{\rm SO}(2))&(\text{Case }1),\\
({\rm Sp}(r,\mathbb{R}),{\rm U}(r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime}))&(\text{Case }2),\\
({\rm SU}(q,s),{\rm S}({\rm U}(q^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\times{\rm U}(q^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime})))&(\text{Case }3),\\
({\rm SO}^{*}(2s),{\rm U}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}))&(\text{Case }4),\\
(E_{7(-25)},{\rm U}(1)\times E_{6(-14)})&(\text{Case }5),\\
(E_{6(-14)},{\rm U}(1)\times{\rm SO}^{*}(10))&(\text{Case }6)\end{cases}

(up to covering).
Let
\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}=:n
,
\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}=:n^{\prime}
,
\dim\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}=:n^{\prime\prime}
,
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}=:r
,
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}=:r^{\prime}
,
\operatorname{rank}\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}=:r^{\prime\prime}
,
and let
d
,
d^{\prime}
,
d^{\prime\prime}
be the numbers defined in (
2.4
) for
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
, respectively.
Then the numbers
(r,r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime},d)
are given by

(r,r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime},d)=\begin{cases}(2,1,1,d)&(\text{Case }1),\\
(r,r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime},1)&(\text{Case }2),\\
(\min\{q,s\},\min\{q^{\prime},s^{\prime}\},\min\{q^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\},2)&(\text{Case }3),\\
(\lfloor s/2\rfloor,\lfloor s^{\prime}/2\rfloor,\lfloor s^{\prime\prime}/2\rfloor,4)&(\text{Case }4),\\
(3,1,2,8)&(\text{Case }5),\\
(2,1,1,6)&(\text{Case }6),\end{cases}

and we have
d=d^{\prime}=d^{\prime\prime}
if
r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime}\neq 1
. Even when
r^{\prime}
or
r^{\prime\prime}=1
, since
d^{\prime}
,
d^{\prime\prime}
are not determined uniquely and any numbers are allowed,
we may assume
d=d^{\prime}=d^{\prime\prime}
.

5.1
Results on weighted Bergman inner products

First, for
f(x_{11})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)
,
g(x_{22})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)
,
we want to compute the top terms and the poles of
\bigl\langle f(x_{11})g(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}
,
where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda}=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\lambda,x}
is the weighted Bergman inner product given in (
2.9
),
with the variable of integration
x=x_{11}+x_{12}+x_{22}
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\operatorname{Re}\lambda>p-1
,
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
, and let
f(x_{11})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)
,
g(x_{22})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)
. Then we have

\bigl\langle f(x_{11})g(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{z_{12}=0}=C_{0}^{d}(\lambda,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})f(z_{11})g(z_{22}),

where

\displaystyle C_{0}^{d}(\lambda,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-1)\right)_{k_{i}+l_{j}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}+1}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}+1}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-2)\right)_{k_{i}+l_{j}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\prod_{a=2}^{r}\prod_{i=\max\{1,a-r^{\prime\prime}\}}^{\min\{a-1,r^{\prime}\}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(a-1)\right)_{k_{i}+l_{a-i}}}{\prod_{a=1}^{r}\prod_{i=\max\{1,a-r^{\prime\prime}\}}^{\min\{a,r^{\prime}+1\}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(a-1)\right)_{k_{i}+l_{a+1-i}}}.

(5.1)

Here we put
k_{r^{\prime}+1}=l_{r^{\prime\prime}+1}:=0
.

Theorem 5.2
.

For
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
, we define
\phi_{0}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}}
by

\displaystyle\phi_{0}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})_{a}:=\min\{k_{i}+l_{j}\mid 1\leq i\leq r^{\prime}+1,\,1\leq j\leq r^{\prime\prime}+1,\,i+j=a+1\},

(5.2)

where
1\leq a\leq r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}
,
k_{r^{\prime}+1}=l_{r^{\prime\prime}+1}:=0
. Then for
f(x_{11})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)
,
g(x_{22})\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)
, as a function of
\lambda
,

(\lambda)_{\phi_{0}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l}),d}\bigl\langle f(x_{11})g(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}

(5.3)

is holomorphically continued for all
\mathbb{C}
.

Remark 5.3
.

(1)

By
[
44
, Corollary 5.7 (3)]
, if
f(x_{11})g(x_{22})\neq 0
and if
\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}}
,
\mathbf{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime\prime}}
satisfy “
\mathbf{k}=\underline{0}_{r^{\prime}}
” or “
\mathbf{l}=\underline{0}_{r^{\prime\prime}}
”
or “
\mathbf{k}=(\underline{k}_{a},\underline{0}_{r^{\prime}-a})
,
1\leq a\leq r^{\prime}
and
l_{a+1}=0
” or “
\mathbf{l}=(\underline{l}_{a},\underline{0}_{r^{\prime\prime}-a})
,
1\leq a\leq r^{\prime\prime}
and
k_{a+1}=0
”,
then (
5.3
) gives a non-zero polynomial in
z\in\mathfrak{p}^{+}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

(2)

The restriction of Theorem
5.2
to
z_{12}=0
, the holomorphy of

(\lambda)_{\phi_{0}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l}),d}\bigl\langle f(x_{11})g(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{z_{12}=0}

follows from Theorem
5.1
, since it is easily proved that
(\lambda)_{\phi_{0}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l}),d}C_{0}^{d}(\lambda,\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})
is holomorphic for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
.

(3)

Under Corollary
2.4
, Theorem
5.2
is equivalent to the inclusion

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{k}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}\bigr)\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{l}}\bigl(\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}\bigr)\subset\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{++}^{r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}}\\
m_{a}\leq\phi_{0}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})_{a}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(\mathfrak{p}^{+}).

(5.4)

When
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
is classical, this follows from (
3.11
), but in the following we prove the theorem by another way, which is available also for the exceptional case.

Here
(\lambda)_{\mathbf{m},d}
is as in (
2.11
), and
\underline{k}_{a}:=(\underbrace{k,\dots,k}_{a})
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

The 2nd equality of (
5.1
) is easy. For the 1st equality, by the last paragraph of Section
3.1
,
we may assume
\mathfrak{p}^{+}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{11}
,
\mathfrak{p}^{+}_{22}
are of tube type, so that
r=r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime}
holds,
and by the
K_{1}
-equivariance, may assume
f(x_{11})=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{11})
,
g(x_{22})=\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22})
.
We prove the theorem by induction on
r^{\prime}
. When
r^{\prime}=0
, this follows from Corollary
2.4
, where we set
\prod_{i=1}^{0}({\cdots})=:1
.
Next we assume the theorem for
r^{\prime}-1
, and prove it for
r^{\prime}
. Since we have defined
k_{r^{\prime}+1}=l_{r^{\prime\prime}+1}=0
, by (
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle\bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda,x}\big|_{z_{12}=0}

\displaystyle\quad{}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z_{11}+z_{22})^{-\lambda+\frac{n}{r}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{22}}\right)^{k_{r^{\prime}}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}}(z_{11}+z_{22})^{\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{n}{r}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}\times\bigl\langle\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r^{\prime}}}(x_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(x_{22}),{\rm e}^{(x|\overline{z})_{\mathfrak{p}^{+}}}\bigr\rangle_{\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}},x}\big|_{z_{12}=0}

\displaystyle\quad{}=\frac{\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}(z_{11})^{k_{r^{\prime}}}}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r},d}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(z_{22})^{-\lambda+\frac{d}{2}(r-1)+1}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{22}}\right)^{k_{r^{\prime}}}\operatorname{det}_{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}(z_{22})^{\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{d}{2}(r-1)-1}

\displaystyle\quad{}\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}\times\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-1)\right)_{k_{i}-k_{r^{\prime}}+l_{j}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}+1}\left(\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-2)\right)_{k_{i}-k_{r^{\prime}}+l_{j}}}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}-\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r^{\prime}}}(z_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(z_{22})

\displaystyle\quad{}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r},d}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-1)\right)_{k_{i}-k_{r^{\prime}}+l_{j}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}+1}\left(\lambda+k_{r^{\prime}}-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-2)\right)_{k_{i}-k_{r^{\prime}}+l_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda+l_{j}-\frac{d}{2}(r^{\prime}+j-1)\right)_{k_{r^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}\times\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(z_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(z_{22})

\displaystyle\quad{}=\frac{1}{(\lambda)_{\underline{k_{r^{\prime}}}_{r},d}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-1)\right)_{k_{i}+l_{j}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}+1}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-2)\right)_{k_{i}+l_{j}}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}+1}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-2)\right)_{k_{r^{\prime}}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{r^{\prime}-1}\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(i+j-1)\right)_{k_{r^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\quad{}\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{}\times\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(r^{\prime}+j-1)\right)_{k_{r^{\prime}}+l_{j}}}{\prod_{j=1}^{r^{\prime\prime}}\left(\lambda-\frac{d}{2}(r^{\prime}+j-1)\right)_{l_{j}}}\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{11}}_{\mathbf{k}}(z_{11})\Delta^{\mathfrak{n}^{+}_{22}}_{\mathbf{l}}(z_{22})
